#2311. Conferenza Gattuso: “Non abbiamo nulla da perdere. Sui tifosi e Zaccagni…"

Alla vigilia di Napoli-Lazio, Gennaro Gattuso è intervenuto in conferenza stampa dal Centro Sportivo di Formello per presentare il match

Alla vigilia di Napoli-Lazio, Gennaro Gattuso è intervenuto in conferenza stampa dal Centro Sportivo di Formello per presentare il match.

CLICCA QUI E SEGUI LAZIOPRESS.IT ANCHE SU INSTAGRAM

Aspettative? Le pressioni specialmente domani non le abbiamo. Abbiamo la consapevolezza di andar a giocare in uno stadio difficile contro una squadra forte di grande mentalità nonostante il periodo difficile. Ci vuole grande rispetto e umiltà, bisogna andare forte. Noi dobbiamo pensare partita dopo partita. Mancano 12 punti, quando arriviamo a 40 punti facciamo una bella festa, il primo obiettivo è stato raggiunto. Abbiamo una strada del lavoro e della mentalità. Ci stanno facendo i complimenti, ma noi dobbiamo lavorare partita dopo partita anche quando arriverà quel giorno.

Spensieratezza e rispetto dell'avversario. Riescono sempre a mettere una squadra competitiva, bisogna vedere i primi 70' ad Oporto, hanno tenuto bene il campo, poi gli sono mancati i cambi. mi aspetto dai miei la voglia di soffrire e stare in campo battendo colpo su colpo. Noi non abbiamo nulla da perdere. Allegri è stato il mio capitano, il mio allenatore, la storia dice che non ho mai vinto contro di lui, spero di fare un quarto di quello che ha fatto nella sua carriera e conoscendolo sono sicuro che lui di stress non muore anzi nei momenti di difficoltà trova lucidità e le cose giuste per far quadrare i conti. Non mi fido perchè sgamato, domani dovremmo essere pronti. Anche per noi sarà una finale.

Sono stato benissimo, abbiamo vinto un trofeo, il rammarico è che ho avuto per poco tempo i tifosi allo stadio causa covid. Mi è rimasta nel cuore, è stato un onore allenare quel club, ho avuto a che fare con persone incredibili che mi assomigliano per come mi piace vivere il quotidiano.

Nuno ha avuto un problema però oggi ha fatto tutto e domani valutiamo, ho un po' di dubbi ancora vediamo. La perdita di Zac è importante, ho letto sei settimane, speriamo di meno, lui ha preso una vecchietta sul quadricipite, abbiamo fatto una risonanza l'edema c'è, un conto è l'edema su una vecchietta un'altra su una lesione. Sta lavorando in piscina per smaltire quest'edema e valutiamo se è un qualcosa di diverso o no, speriamo possano esserci note positive. Gudmundsson non va in Nazionale, settimana prossima abbiamo un amichevole e l'obiettivo è fargli fare 40', Rovella e Marusic domani vengono con noi.

Quando vai a pressare bene le squadre vanno in verticale, come fai a recuperare palla, questo dato mi fa ridere. A me interessa il baricentro, se rubiamo palle là andiamo in gol sistematicamente.

Se vogliamo commettere l'errore domani è andare là e fare una passeggiata. Non sta nemmeno a me ricordarlo ai giocatori, sono stati i primi i miei giocatori a leggere che il Napoli è in ritiro e che per loro è una finale. Questa settimana abbiamo spinto, ci sono state due sedute molto dure ma i giocatori sono pronti e ora bisogna dimostrarlo domani.

Marusic a differenza di Albert è pronto per fare l'allenatore ha una grande mentalità, sicuramente può andare a mettere minuti nelle gambe, secondo me ha senso, ha giudizio. Noi vediamo i dati, li sento, confrontiamo i dati ma non stiamo là a dire di lavorare meno, non mi permetto. Noi lunedì martedì e mercoledì avremo liberi.

Oggi Zac mangerà con noi, partirà con noi, verrà alla stazione a prendersi l'incitamento, poi torna a casa e domani partirà con la macchina per essere in spogliatoio con noi, l'ha voluto lui non gliel'ho chiesto io.

L'altro giorno chi è entrato ha cambiato la partita, è un valore aggiunto. Dispiace aver perso Zaccagni, bisogna essere pronti bravi e preparati. Pinamonti sta meglio, sta cominciando a ripulire bene i palloni, ad attaccare la profondità, è diverso da Noslin, ma ci può dare una grandissima mano, c'è grandissimo rispetto nei suoi confronti. È un ragazzo un po' taciturno, ma quando sta bene ti può dare una grandissima mano, si fa voler bene dai compagni, sta cominciando a farci vedere robe che piacciono a noi.

Non a livello ambientale, ma come gioco. Se analizzi loro cambiano molto, sono arrivato in ritardo per questo motivo qua per far capire ai ragazzi cosa possono fare. Loro possono partire a 3 o 4. Dobbiamo pensare a noi stessi, dobbiamo essere bravi noi a capire subito, non deve mancare voglia e veemenza.

Non ha senso, quando vogliamo fare sedute una-due volte a settimana, la quantità e la qualità del lavoro che proponiamo bisogna stare bene sennò non è allenante, sei troppo a rischio. Su Marusic e Rovella abbiamo cercato di farli entrare quando avevano sulle gambe quel tipo di lavoro, sennò c'è rischio delle ricadute. Se chiedi di stringere i denti e dopo ha una ricaduta il calciatore, il rapporto si rovina. Nuno non stava benissimo settimana scorsa e i dati ce lo dicevano, mi sono fatto convincere però ha spinto poco, chapeau a lui che is è messo a disposizione, ma se vuoi giocare in un certo modo c'è bisogno di gente che sta bene.

Ho parlato con Derkum, che è quello che ha tutti i numeri e ad oggi i numeri dicono che le presenze e gli anni sarà Isaksen.

Deve essere bravo, c'è gente che bisogna stimolarla invece a lui dobbiamo essere bravi a fargli capire che una buona gestione non sarebbe male. Lui non si risparmia mai.

Un messaggio d'amore, ci stanno vicini. Un messaggio di grandissima coerenza, ci deve dare quella carica in più, di riuscire a portare un messaggio positivo. È un orgoglio che fa piacere. Li ringrazio a nome die ragazzi e dello staff, bisogna renderli orgogliosi con le prestazioni e il modo di onorare la maglia.

Io non ci penso, dobbiamo pensare fare le cose nostre. Sono 4 settimane che dico che noi non c'entriamo nulla là sopra. Giochiamola e viviamola senza nessuna preoccupazione, dobbiamo giocare le partite con mentalità. Ti aspettavi che noi eravamo là? No, e allora perchè dobbiamo appesantirci, giochiamo con rispetto contro una squadra forte che sta vivendo un momento negativo, andiamocela a giocare con voglia ma senza con l'idea di vincere perchè siamo primi. Noi dobbiamo pensare a cosa dobbiamo fare. Se cominciamo a pensare al primato sbagliamo tutto, poi arriva una mazzata e pensiamo che è tutto da buttare.

#2310. Lazio wow, Belahyane ricorda: "Molta gente pensava saremmo stati da zona retrocessione"

Questa stagione è la rinascita di Reda Belahyane alla Lazio. Diciotto mesi dopo il suo trasferimento dall'Hellas Verona, il centrocampista marocchino ha ottenuto la chiamata in nazionale maggiore due anni dopo la sua ultima convocazione grazie alle prestazioni offerte sotto la guida di Gennaro Gattuso. Adesso il 22enne comanda la classifica di Serie A con 28 punti dopo 10 giornate, prima di sfidare il Napoli domani (12:30).

Anche per la Lazio nella sua storia. Una sorpresa per i giocatori biancocelesti, Belahyane incluso: "Sì. Vista la scorsa stagione (nono posto), nessuno avrebbe scommesso su di noi", ha sottolineato. "È storico ma noi, i giocatori, non guardiamo la classifica. Cerchiamo di sfuggire all'entusiasmo ambientale. La cosa più importante è che continuiamo a lavorare". La formazione di Gattuso è l'unica dei cinque grandi campionati europei a non aver ancora perso, insieme a Bayern Monaco e Borussia Dortmund: davvero la salvezza è l'obiettivo stagionale? Belahyane risponde subito: "Pensiamo a un po' più di questo adesso. Con tutti i problemi del club, il braccio di ferro tra i tifosi e la dirigenza, il blocco del mercato, molta gente pensava che saremmo stati nella zona retrocessione. È bello farli ricredere. Cerchiamo davvero di andarci con tutta la nostra aggressività, il nostro coraggio".

Nicolò Rovella si è infortunato nuovamente contro il Genoa (1-0), il 30 agosto, ma Belahyane si è fatto trovare pronto per coprire il vuoto lasciato dal compagno di squadra in regia. "È soprattutto grazie all'arrivo dell'allenatore", confida l'ex Verona sul ruolo avuto da Gattuso. "Mi ha dato molta fiducia. È qualcuno che fa venire voglia di lottare per lui. Ci trasmette la rabbia che aveva da giocatore. Ed è capace di mantenere tutto un gruppo coinvolto. Ascolta tutti". Poi ha proseguito nella spiegazione dell'effetto Rino: "Ci trasmette la rabbia che aveva da giocatore. Poi, è capace di mantenere tutto un gruppo coinvolto. È qualcuno che ascolta tutti e che non guarda le persone dall'alto in basso. Non ti lascia a rimuginare in panchina senza mai parlarti. Sente se qualcosa non va. Trova del buono in ogni giocatore e senti che è sincero".

In pratica Gattuso mette d'accordo tutto e per Belahyane è l'allenatore che serviva: "Penso di sì (sorride, ndr). Speravo di giocare da molto tempo, due allenatori non mi hanno dato fiducia (Marco Baroni e Maurizio Sarri, ndr), non mi adattavo ai loro principi di gioco. Dopo un po', la fiducia se ne va, si inizia a dubitare. Gattuso mi ha rimesso in campo, mi ha fatto prendere coscienza che non ero lì per caso". Invece, sul ruolo di regista adottato in biancoceleste questa stagione, il marocchino di 22 anni spiega: "Mi piace questo ruolo, anche se passa sotto i radar. Quando ho ricominciato a giocare, avevo quel piccolo stress che accompagna i giocatori che hanno giocato poco. Allora mi rifugiavo nel recupero palla. Ma se mi allontanavo dal gioco, l'allenatore mi urlava dietro: 'No, vieni a giocare'. Oggi la fiducia è tornata. Cerco di conservare i palloni, di distribuirli per avviare le azioni". Fin dove potrà spingersi questa Lazio? "Siamo performanti e io inanello partite, ma attenzione, siamo appena a tre mesi di competizione e in fondo sono come la squadra in questo momento, fuggo un po' dall'entusiasmo ambientale", la risposta di Belahyane.

#2308. Martin O'Neill gushes over 'class' Celtic star after European heroics

Camilo Duran earned glowing praise from Martin O’Neill after coming off the bench to fire Celtic to a dramatic Europa League victory over Besiktas.

The Colombian struck twice in a thrilling 3-2 win at Celtic Park as the Hoops finally got off the mark in the competition on their fourth attempt.

Duran was introduced alongside Callum Osmand and Colby Donovan shortly after the hour mark with Celtic trailing 1-0 and struggling to seriously threaten the visitors. The changes transformed the contest.

Osmand equalised with his first touch just 49 seconds after entering the action before setting up Duran to put Celtic ahead. Besiktas hit back to make it 2-2, but Duran struck again late on to complete his double and seal a huge European victory.

O’Neill was delighted with the impact of the forward and revealed he had always intended to introduce him from the bench. “Duran has been fabulous for us all season and got his two goals,” O’Neill said.

“He has played a lot of games and looked a bit tired in the last couple. But he’s a class player, and he was always going to come on.

“I’m delighted with the performance. It was fantastic.”

The victory brought back memories of O’Neill’s first spell in charge of Celtic as a raucous home crowd roared their side to victory. “That was a special European night,” he said. “It reminded me of the olden days.

“The subs re-energised us and played a big part in the win.”

Martin O'Neill reveals Celtic masterplan behind game-changing subs

Martin O'Neill fires 'a lot more' Celtic demand at £3.5m signing

The telling Michael Nicholson quip that reveals Celtic's transfer strategy

O’Neill was particularly pleased with the character his players showed after falling behind and then being pegged back at 2-2 late in the game. “I told the players afterwards that they are a credit to the club,” he added.

“You can’t get a bigger accolade than that, but we put our heart and soul into the game. We played really good stuff, particularly in the second half.

"At 2-2, it looked like we would drop two points but we came roaring back to win the game. There was so much spirit and desire to get the three points. The character shone through big time.

"The winners came to the fore, as did the new boys.”

Duran also earned praise from fellow substitute Osmand following his match-winning display. “Camilo did amazing; he’s such a poacher,” He said. “Credit to him.

“The team dug in, stayed strong and we got our rewards. I was so happy to come on, score a goal and get the win.”

The victory gave Celtic renewed hope of progressing in the Europa League ahead of their meeting with Portuguese second-tier side Torreense on November 26. O’Neill said: “We rose to the occasion and we have given ourselves a chance.

“We are off the mark now. We have points on the board, and we can park it for a few weeks now. There is still a thought about qualifying, but that is a long way off.

“Hopefully it gives us a platform to charge on, but it’s not going to be easy.”

#2307. Davis è lo specchio della crisi dell'Udinese. L'ultima gioia personale è un ricordo lontano

I numeri di Keinan Davis fotografano a pieno la crisi dell'Udinese, ultima in classifica. L'attaccante inglese è ancora a quota zero reti in campionato, dopo 10 partite giocate. E il gol fallito da pochi passi al "Penzo" sullo 0-0 spiega benissimo lo stato di crisi del giocatore e della squadra, entrata in un buco nero dalla quale non riesce più a uscire. Per rendere l'idea dell'eccezionalità della situazione basti pensare che l'ultima volta in cui i friulani erano soli all'ultimo posto risale a 16 anni fa, ottobre 2010. Nelle ultime nove giornate la squadra è letteralmente crollata, con un punto nelle ultime 9 giornate.

Spesso tradito dai problemi fisici, Davis quest'anno sta trovando continuità: una sola partita saltata, quella dell'esordio contro il Como. Poi 9 partite su 10 da titolare. 688 minuti complessivi senza gol, peggio di lui fra gli altri attaccanti il solo El Bilal Touré del Parma a quota 764'. Per ritrovare l'ultimo gol dell'inglese in Serie A dobbiamo risalire a quasi otto mesi fa, quando chiudeva i conti a "Marassi" in un brillante 0-2 sul Genoa. Una stagione che sotto il profilo realizzativo era stata la sua migliore, per la prima volta in doppia cifra.

Paolo Zanetti punta su di lui. A fine gara ha dichiarato: "Ha fatto una buona partita, ha avuto due-tre occasioni e se avesse segnato magari sarebbe cambiato qualcosa". Il centravanti dal canto suo ha mostrato particolare attaccamento alla causa, stringendo i denti nell'ultimo periodo e scendendo in campo a Venezia nonostante non fosse al top della condizione.

Pesa soprattutto l'assenza di alternative: Zaniolo è un trequartista/seconda punta, Gueye è fermo da metà settembre e Bayo è rientrato proprio nell'ultima partita dopo aver saltato per infortunio le precedenti cinque. La pausa nazionali servirà in questo caso a sistemare le cose.

#2306. James Morrison issues firm statement over West Brom star’s Premier League potential

West Brom missed the opportunity to move into the Championship’s top two after being held to a 1-1 draw by Southampton on Tuesday night. James Morrison’s side now turn their attention to this weekend’s clash with Millwall, with the Baggies preparing to host the Lions in a bid to strengthen their promotion push.

Ahead of this weekend’s clash, Morrison has been asked about his midfielder Ousmane Diakite, who has been in excellent form this season. The WBA boss was questioned on Diakite’s potential, specifically whether he is a Premier League player in the making, as reported by Steve Tudgay.

Morrison issued a confident response, saying, “Yes he is. With us!” His comments back up those of former WBA man Paul Robinson, who was full of praise for Diakite when speaking to the Express and Star.

“He has Premier League attributes, when you look at him,” the former defender said. “It’s about consistency. We have seen Diakite in the Blackburn game, then the Preston game and now he has gone to that next level against West Ham.

“He stood out against players who are expected to win the league. He destroyed them in the middle of the park.”

Diakite has made an encouraging start to the 2026/27 Championship season, establishing himself as an important figure in the WBA midfield. The 26-year-old has featured in 14 league games, making 11 starts and averaging a FotMob rating of 7.22.

His biggest strengths have been his defensive contributions, recording 25 tackles, 19 interceptions and 58 recoveries. He has also won 81.8% of his aerial duels, placing him in the 95th percentile among midfielders.

Diakite has shown composure in possession too, completing 88.5% of his 544 passes, including 86 line-breaking efforts. His recent performances have been particularly impressive, earning ratings of 7.8 against Southampton and Blackburn and 7.7 against Preston.

Although he is yet to register a goal or assist, his physicality, defensive reliability and ability to retain possession have made him a valuable asset for West Brom.

Morrison is building an exciting, well-balanced midfield at West Brom, combining defensive strength, energy and attacking quality. Diakite and Jayson Molumby provide a solid foundation, while summer signing Felix Horn Myhre adds creativity and goals.

Isaac Price has also emerged as a key attacking threat, already contributing five goals this season. There is promising young talent in Rabby Nzingoula, Toby Collyer and Harry Whitwell, too, giving Morrison options for the future.

The group has helped West Brom make a strong start to the campaign, with its blend of experience and potential offering encouragement for a sustained promotion push.

#2304. World media reacts as Besiktas tears flow after ‘worst Celtic team ever’ roar back

For a while on Bonfire Night, this threatened to be another familiar continental tale, with Celtic’s knockout hopes going up in smoke.

Jordan Lotomba conceded a second-half penalty, with Sam Johnstone saving from Junior Olaitan, only for the striker to bury the rebound.

But O’Neill’s changes transformed the night. Callum Osmand needed just 43 seconds to level with his first touch before Camilo Duran rounded the keeper to complete the turnaround.

Read more: Martin O'Neill gushes over 'class' Celtic star after European heroics

Orkun Kokcu’s stunning strike made it 2-2 and threatened another European gut punch.

Duran scrambled home from close range to spark bedlam and finally get that continental monkey off Celtic’s back.

Whether it ignites an unlikely charge towards the knockouts remains to be seen.

And the big win in Glasgow sparked the usual calm and measured verdicts in Turkey. Yeah, right.

In NBA games, at critical moments, the fans shout, ‘Defence! Defence!’ Beşiktaş were crying out for half-time in the same way. I would have taken Agbadou off immediately. A great football player, according to some. According to whom? He made a clear mistake against Kasımpaşa and there was no problem today because Celtic couldn't get beyond midfield. I watched the worst Celtic I have ever seen in my life in that first half. How did you manage to turn the game into this when there was nothing happening?

Beşiktaş learned nothing from the Kasımpaşa match. They just sat back! When they took the lead away from home, they acted like the match was over. It continued exactly as before. They scored the goal, then just stood there watching. Italiano's team, which had been superior in every aspect until that moment, surrendered. It was unacceptable to bow down to Celtic, who are falling apart in Europe.

If the opening period was cagey, the second half was a rip-roaring rollercoaster. Junior Olaitan put the visitors ahead after Sam Johnstone had saved his initial penalty, and then it became the Callum Osmand and Camilo Durán show, with the former equalising and the latter scoring a double either side of Orkun Kökçü's howitzer to give Celtic their first win of the campaign.

Camilo Durán struck twice off the bench, including an 87th-minute winner, as Celtic came from behind to beat Beşiktaş 3-2 at Celtic Park in Glasgow on Thursday in the UEFA Europa League. A crowd of 44,394 watched Celtic fall behind after 54 minutes before equalising, taking the lead, being pegged back and then watching Durán decide the match late on.

The loudest cheers in the first half came at the expense of former Rangers player Cerny, whose previous visit to Celtic Park ended with him being spoken to by police after squirting liquid at home fans while celebrating a goal. The home fans were briefly lifted by a heavy tackle from Liam Scales on the Czech winger and then by his wayward strike.

#2303. Samp, D'Angelo: "Settimana proficua, i ragazzi hanno fatto un buonissimo lavoro"

Continuità. E' quello che cerca la Sampdoria dopo il successo casalingo nel derby ligure contro l'Entella. In vista della sfida contro la Carrarese di domani alle 17.15 il tecnico blucerchiato Luca D'Angelo è intervenuto in conferenza stampa dal centro sportivo "Gloriano Mugnaini" di Bogliasco.

Cosa aspettarsi dalla gara contro la Carrarese? "Abbiamo ottenuto una vittoria in casa che ha dato morale. Andiamo ad affrontare una partita non facile come lo sono tutte in Serie B ma i ragazzi hanno fatto un buonissimo lavoro in settimana anche se abbiamo avuto la problematica dei giocatori fuori. Agli infortunati si è aggiunto anche lo squalificato Cicconi".

TMW - Insigne ed Esposito saranno a disposizione? "Non saranno della gara e li aspettiamo per dopo la sosta".

TMW - Costa Abildgaard? "Abildgaard oggi si è allenato, avrò molto di parlarne e sembrava sciolto e vediamo se convocarlo".

Carrara non sarà un campo facile. "Può influire ma non deve essere una scusante per noi. Alla fine in campo saranno undici giocatori loro e undici noi. Saranno più abituati a giocare in quel campo ma possiamo fare una bella partita".

Saranno comunque convocati Esposito e Insigne per fare gruppo? "Esposito forse sì ma Insigne forse no, dovrà farsi trovare pronto per dopo la sosta".

Continuerà con la difesa a tre e a quattro? "In questo momento abbiamo lavorato soltanto sulla difesa a quattro. E continueremo su quella linea".

Mezzotero è reduce dal primo gol in carriera? "Ha fatto una buonissima partita, si sta allenando molto bene. Gli attaccanti sono tre: lui, Karamoh e Tutino. O dall'inizio o a gara in corso ci sarà spazio per tutti e tre".

Come hai gestito l'entusiasmo di Mezzotero? "Bisogna allenarsi forte in settimana e farsi trovare pronti in partita. Lui lo sta facendo. L'aspetto mediatico può coincidere ma fa parte della crescita del giocatore".

Migliorare l'aspetto offensivo? "Dobbiamo migliorare da quel punto di vista ma soprattutto nel reparto trequartisti e attaccanti abbiamo maggiori defezioni. Dobbiamo stringerlo il campo e accorciarlo. Con il tempo e il rientro dei tanti giocatori troveremo giovamento".

Laritsen non sarà della partita? "Ha la tonsillite e non riesce ad allenarsi".

TMW - Ha avuto la possibilità per la prima volta di lavorare tutta la settimana con la squadra. "La settimana stata proficua da quel punto di vista. Il numero esiguo di giocatori ti mette nella situazione di andare meno nello specifico. Il ritmo va modulato perché non possiamo permetterci di perdere giocatori negli allenamenti. Dobbiamo andare però oltre questa difficoltà".

Giocherà Gartenmann al posto di Cicconi? "Può essere quella o anche Mbabu a sinistra con Gartmenmann a destra. Ho provato anche Diop, giocatore su cui punto maggiormente. Mi ha colpito la sua fisicità e in un calcio di aggressività lui è già pronto".

#2302. Liverpool transfer news: Reds in ‘advanced talks’ over new contract for player ‘trusted’ by Andoni Iraola

By Oliver Thomas | 05 Nov 2026 12:02

Liverpool have reportedly entered advanced negotiations with teenage starlet Trey Nyoni over a new contract.

The Reds suffered a disappointing 3-2 Champions League defeat away against Fenerbahce on Wednesday, but it was a memorable night for Nyoni who scored his first goal for the club.

At the age of 19 years and 127 days, Nyoni became the youngest player in the club's history to score his first goal for the team in a European Cup match, while he is Liverpool’s second youngest Champions League goalscorer after Trent Alexander-Arnold.

Nyoni has stepped up to Andoni Iraola’s first team this season after the Reds rejected a £30m offer from Nottingham Forest during the summer transfer window.

The academy graduate was handed his first Premier League start for Liverpool in their 1-0 defeat to champions Arsenal at Anfield last weekend and has played 12 times for the Reds across all competitions this term.

Nyoni has made a notable impression in Iraola’s midfield playing alongside either Alexis Mac Allister, Dominik Szoboszlai or Ryan Gravenberch, and the youngster could soon be rewarded with an improved deal.

According to The Athletic, Nyoni is in advanced talks with Liverpool over extending his contract, even though his current deal runs until June 2030.

The report adds that Nyoni has been discussing his future with Liverpool for 'months', with the club keen to reward him with fresh terms to reflect his first-team emergence under Iraola.

While a contract extension has yet to be finalised, it is understood that there is optimism that an agreement will be reached, with a breakthrough in negotiations expected soon.

Speaking to reporters before Liverpool’s loss to Fenerbahce, Iraola made it clear that he believes Nyoni is now “in a very good place” to enhance his development.

“Trey just has to continue in the same way as since the beginning of pre-season, be ready for all of the games,” said the Spaniard.

“We trust him, he’s doing very well. We have to encourage him to continue learning, improving and getting experience. But he’s in a very good place.”

With Gravenberch facing a spell on the sidelines through injury and Szoboszlai having only just recovered from an injury of his own, Nyoni is a strong contender to start again for Liverpool in Sunday’s Premier League clash against Crystal Palace.

#2301. Fortuna Sittard vs Telstar - prediction, team news, lineups

By Adepoju Marvellous | 05 Nov 2026 12:26

High-flying Fortuna Sittard return to Eredivisie action this weekend, hosting Telstar at the Fortuna Sittard Stadion on Saturday.

The home side begin this weekend’s round of fixtures in sixth place with 19 points - 10 places and 10 points clear of the visitors.

A trip to Feyenoord, who had won three straight meetings between the teams, threatened to end Fortuna’s six-game unbeaten run, especially after Luciano Valente opened the scoring just before the hour mark. However, Shiloh ‘t Zand equalised with four minutes remaining to force a share of the spoils.

While some may argue the point flattered Danny Buijs’s men given their performance, it was a reward for their resilience, a quality they have displayed in abundance since a 5-1 drubbing at the hands of Ajax in mid-September.

Having collected just seven points from a possible 15 in Sittard-Geleen, Fortuna’s return to form still has room for improvement via consistent results at home. A 1-0 win over Excelsior in their last home outing suggests they are moving in the right direction.

Bottom of the league in average ball possession this term (35.7%), Fortuna remain a side best known for their off-the-ball work. They finished second-bottom in this metric last season, only ahead of relegated Heracles.

With each of their next six matches coming against teams currently below them in the table, Fortuna have an opportunity to build a sustained run of positive results and enter the second half of the season well positioned for a European push.

After almost three months, Telstar finally ended their wait for a first victory since beating NEC Nijmegen on the opening weekend, running out emphatic 4-2 winners over FC Utrecht in Sunday’s clash at BUKO Stadion.

The victory came at a crucial time for manager Henk Brugge, who was under increased scrutiny after his side were knocked out of the KNVB Beker first round by lower-league opposition Almere City just four days earlier.

Two points adrift of safety, another Telstar defeat this weekend could pile the pressure back on Brugge and their away record this season has been dismal, with just one win, four points, and four goals scored from five matches on the road.

However, De Witte Leeuwen are unbeaten in their last five trips to the Fortuna Sittard Stadion dating back to August 2014, and will draw confidence from their most recent trip in March, when they cruised to a 4-1 victory.

Facing a side with the best big-chance conversion rate in the division (76.9%), Saturday’s visitors will be wary of conceding too many opportunities, despite boasting a significant advantage in clear-cut chances created (23-10).

Rodrigo Guth and Lequincio Zeefuik both limped off with injuries in Fortuna’s last outing and are doubtful for Saturday’s clash.

This could open the door for Ivan Marquez and Kristoffer Peterson - both substitutes last time out - to come into the hosts’ starting lineup.

Twenty-one-year-old Gerald Alders has missed Telstar’s last three outings and remains a doubt for this weekend.

Patrick Brouwer and Rui Mendes were also absent against Utrecht, although the former is likely to return to action in Sittard.

Tyrone Owusu has scored five goals and provided one assist in his last five matches for the visitors, making the midfielder one to watch.

Fortuna Sittard possible starting lineup:

Branderhorst; Pinto, Van Ottele, Marquez, Hubner, Dahlhaus; Ihattaren, Descotte, Brittijn, ‘t Zand; Zeefuik

Koeman Jr; Peersman, Soualhia, Valk, Caschili; Rossen, Ahmed; Demircan, Owusu, Martha; Thorisson

Fortuna are playing their best football in some time, and that momentum makes them strong favourites to edge an out-of-sorts Telstar side.

We expect the home side to make light work of their visitors and head into the international break on a high.

#2300. Sean Neave won Wolves the game against Bristol City, and he can do the same vs Wrexham: Opinion

Wolves staged an impressive fightback to defeat Bristol City on Wednesday night. Scott Twine put the Robins ahead, and it appeared they would be securing a valuable three-points against an in-form Wanderers side.

However, Cesar Peixoto would introduce Sean Neave in place of Fer Lopez on the 73rd minute, and the move changed the game. First, the Newcastle United loanee pounced on the rebound to level the scores, before meeting Ladislav Krejci’s header back across the box to slide home and win it for the Old Gold with a minute to spare.

The goals were Neave’s first and second for Wolves, and in senior football. The 19-year-old has offered some bright performances off the bench to date, and the traits that helped him win the Bristol City game could see him play a key role against Wrexham this Saturday too.

Neave is a raw and developing young striker. He had only played two times for Newcastle United’s first-team before joining Wolves, but a prolific record at U21 (19 goals in 36 games) and U18 (21 goals in 37 games) means he comes highly regarded from his parent club.

For the most part, Neave has been used as an impact player from the bench. He started and assisted in the win over Burnley and played the first 45 minutes against Portsmouth in the following game.

However, at the moment, it feels he is most effective as an impact sub, and the Bristol City game showed that. Having watched Neave closely at both Wolves and in his Newcastle United development, he has a great burst of pace to pounce on loose balls and run beyond the defensive line.

This was on show in his goals against Bristol City – first pouncing on a rebound before meeting a header at the back-post to tap in his second. In between those two goals, he got the jump on the Robins defence to run in behind before guiding an effort wide off the inside of his foot.

Neave’s sharpness, speed and quick movement will be a worry for Wrexham. A lot of their centre-backs are strong, but they do not have the speed to deal with Neave.

By either playing in balls for Neave to run onto or subbing him on late against tired legs, he can expose the lack of pace among the likes of Joe Worrall, Max Cleworth or Dom Hyam.

Game time is key for the development of all young players, and Neave arrived at Wolves knowing he would have to earn his opportunities. Raul Jimenez joined to lead the line, while Adam Armstrong has a fantastic record at Championship level.

However, Jimenez’s injury means Neave has found opportunities, and he’s taking them. The brace against Bristol City will hopefully be an exciting sign of what’s to come from the teenage striker – both over the course of this season and in his career.

Neave will be taking it one step at a time, but just as he did against the Robins, he can be a thorn in the side of Wrexham’s defence with his pace and movement on Saturday.

#2299. Rangers star wanted for permanent transfer as current boss makes feelings clear

Jim Goodwin admits Dundee United would jump at the chance to sign Lyall Cameron permanently from Rangers.

The midfielder has impressed since moving to Tannadice on loan, establishing himself as an important part of Goodwin’s side.

Cameron joined Rangers from Dundee in the summer of 2025 but struggled to establish himself as a regular at Ibrox. He spent the second half of last season on loan at Aberdeen before joining Dundee United on another temporary spell.

Cameron remains under contract with Rangers until 2029, meaning his parent club retain control over his long-term future. However, Goodwin has made no secret of his desire to keep the midfielder if an opportunity arises.

“I think we’d be stupid not to explore something like that, if it were to be possible,” he said. “We also need to be realistic. Lyall is on a long-term contract with Rangers, so, ultimately, he is their player, and I’m sure Derek McInnes is watching the way he is performing with interest.

“I’m sure there would also be a whole host of clubs interested if Lyall were to become available. However, I think we’ve potentially put ourselves in a good position.

“He is loving being at Dundee United; he feels at home here and knows exactly what we think of him. He’s already a very important player for us.

“If the opportunity was there, I’m sure we’d look to make him a good offer. But, as I say, he’s a Rangers player and it’s far too early to say what might happen.

“The main thing is that Lyall is enjoying his football and continues to do well; we’ll enjoy him while he’s here.”

#2298. Icardi vuole la Juve. Quanto ha segnato e quanto guadagnava in Turchia? Spoiler: tanto

Il nome di Mauro Icardi - sussurrano in Argentina - è tornato a circolare con insistenza dalle parti della Continassa. La Juventus cerca un centravanti per aumentare il proprio peso offensivo e Maurito, rimasto senza squadra dopo l'addio al Galatasaray, rappresenta un'occasione che merita attenzione. A favorire l'operazione c'è soprattutto Luciano Spalletti, che conosce molto bene le caratteristiche del numero 9 e lo ha già allenato ai tempi dell'Inter. Icardi, dal canto suo, sarebbe disposto a ridimensionare sensibilmente le proprie pretese economiche pur di tornare in Serie A. Ma quanto ha segnato e quanto guadagnava il classe '93 in Turchia?

I numeri della parentesi turca raccontano un attaccante che, nonostante il lungo stop per la rottura del crociato, ha saputo mantenere negli anni il feeling con il gol. Nelle quattro stagioni con il Galatasaray, Icardi ha collezionato infatti 134 presenze, realizzando ben 77 reti e servendo 25 assist. Un bottino notevole, impreziosito da quattro campionati turchi, una Coppa nazionale e una Supercoppa. Soltanto nell'ultima annata ha messo insieme 47 apparizioni, 16 gol e 3 assist: un biglietto da visita tutt'altro che trascurabile per chi cerca un "usato sicuro" in area di rigore.

Resta però da affrontare il capitolo economico. Al Galatasaray Icardi percepiva uno stipendio fisso di 6 milioni di euro netti a stagione, come comunicato ufficialmente dal club al momento del trasferimento a titolo definitivo nel 2023. A questa cifra si aggiungevano i bonus, che secondo la stampa turca portavano potenzialmente il compenso complessivo fino a circa 10 milioni. Un'intesa difficilmente replicabile, per ovvie ragioni, nel campionato italiano. Ecco perché l'eventuale ritorno in Serie A passerebbe necessariamente da una revisione dell'ingaggio. La Juventus osserva, Spalletti conosce il suo 9, la suggestione potrebbe trasformarsi in qualcosa di più concreto nelle prossime settimane.

#2296. Chicago Fire vs Columbus Crew - prediction, team news, lineups

By Byron David | 05 Nov 2026 12:43, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 20:40

Decision Day in MLS sees Chicago Fire searching for home advantage in the Best-of-Three series when they host Columbus Crew at Soldier Field on Saturday, November 7.

The visitors have every incentive to win the final regular-season match, as it would help them avoid the single-elimination wild card match.

Chicago have already done better than their regular-season performance from last term, as they cannot finish below fifth, which is three spots better than 2025.

The Fire will hope to go a step further, though, after the Philadelphia Union bundled them out in the Best-of-Three series last season.

Home advantage will be crucial for them going forward, but they must make it count on the final day to secure Soldier Field as the destination for the next phase of the playoffs.

Gregg Berhalter's charges are now unbeaten across their last three MLS fixtures, but they have not won any of their previous two games that they have hosted.

The Fire won the corresponding fixture last season, and avoided defeat in the reverse match earlier this term, but the visitors have a better head-to-head record.

Nonetheless, the hosts must win here to control their own destiny, as even a draw may not be enough for them to secure home advantage in the next round.

Alternatively, Chicago fans can quietly get behind Inter Miami, who will be entertaining Charlotte at the same time, as if the Herons win or draw with the Crown, then the Fire will be comfortably through to the next round, with the first game set to be at Soldier Field.

Columbus Crew have qualified for the playoffs in some form, but as things stand, they must contest the one-off elimination match.

Laurent Courtois will hope that his men can make a play for a Best-of-Three series spot with a win in Illinois.

The Black and Gold have won their last five MLS matches in a row, which means they will head into this fixture full of confidence.

The fact that they won three of the last four head-to-head matches in Chicago will further inspire them to cause an upset this weekend.

The visitors will secretly hope for Nashville to beat or draw with FC Cincinnati, which will open their path to the next phase of the competition.

It is mostly good news for the hosts as they head into the most important part of the MLS season, with South African defender Mbekezeli Mbokazi returning from injury.

Robert Lewandowski snapped an eight-game goal drought last weekend, when he came off the bench to hand his team the lead in their 4-2 win away to Colorado Rapids.

The Polish international then turned creator for Puso Dithejane to add some glitter to the scoreline, but he will certainly be important for Berhalter's men.

Another man that the home side will depend on is Ivorian Jonathan Bamba, who scored a brace last week, taking his goal contributions this season to 12.

The Fire will be without Robin Lod and Sam Rogers, who are both injured.

The Crew are sweating over the fitness of Brais Mendez, who has been excellent since joining as a Designated Player from Real Sociedad.

Wessam Abou Ali and Rudy Camacho are definitely out of contention for the trip to Chicago.

Dylan Chambost will be crucial for the visitors' creativity, as he has done recently for the Crew.

#2295. England squad: Choose Thomas Tuchel's 26 players for Spain, Croatia fixtures

By Ben Knapton | 05 Nov 2026 12:47

A spot in the UEFA Nations League knockout stages is on the line for England when they complete their League A campaign against Spain and Croatia during the November international break.

Thomas Tuchel's side kicked off Group 3 on a sour note with a 3-2 home defeat to the world champions, but back-to-back victories over the Czech Republic and a 7-0 crushing of Croatia on the road have put them on the brink of progression.

The Three Lions just need one point from their final two games to progress to the quarter-finals, and here, Sports Mole gives you the chance to pick your England squad for the November international break - be sure to download and share with your friends!

Tuchel should have no major quandaries on the goalkeeping front, as James Trafford and Jordan Pickford are shoo-ins, while Dean Henderson is likely to displace Brighton & Hove Albion veteran Jason Steele after recovering from injury.

However, the England boss now faces the age-old Ben White dilemma in defence, as the Arsenal right-back has stayed injury-free in recent weeks and has enjoyed a strong start to the campaign with the Premier League champions.

White has provided three assists in 12 games for the Gunners while starting seven of their nine top-flight fixtures, but he has not been selected for England since March; a hamstring injury ruled him out of the extended September/October period.

Tuchel must also weigh up whether to recall Harry Maguire, who has been a stalwart in an under-performing Manchester United side, and Djed Spence, who has forced his way into the Inter Milan XI after recovering from injury.

The biggest dilemma for the former Chelsea head coach undoubtedly comes in the final third of the field, though, as all eyes will be on whether Blues playmaker Cole Palmer is included in Tuchel's ranks.

Palmer pulled out of the September/October squad through injury, triggering a frustrated response from Tuchel, who admitted that the 24-year-old misses too many camps and his England future is potentially in jeopardy under the current regime.

However, a fully-fit Palmer could be offered a route back into the squad due to Bukayo Saka's injury, as the Arsenal winger is expected to be out until December with the quadriceps problem he sustained against Bayern Munich in the Champions League.

Noni Madueke and Phil Foden will also be fighting for attacking recalls, although the latter - who was also snubbed from the World Cup group - has already suggested that the November squad may come a little too soon for him.

Madueke, on the other hand, may be directly competing with Arsenal teammate Eberechi Eze, who was included in the September/October selection despite his fringe status at the Emirates.

Meanwhile, Dominic Calvert-Lewin was summoned to play second fiddle to Harry Kane last month, but having scored just once in his last five Premier League games, the Leeds United striker's spot could come under threat from Ollie Watkins.

#2294. The Millwall team Alex Neil should start vs West Brom to recover from 3-0 midweek defeat

Millwall were well-beaten on Tuesday night as Birmingham City scored three goals inside 30 minutes to secure the victory. The loss sees the Lions pick up just four points from their last five games.

Alex Neil’s side had an xG greater than BCFC’s at 1.42, but errors cost them any potential result. The South London outfit were no match for the Blues’ superior defence, who have conceded just 13 times this term.

Heading to The Hawthorns on Saturday, Millwall need to pick up a result. With just one point per game on average on the road this campaign, the Lions have a lot to do against a West Brom side who are averaging 1.79 points from their last five matches.

Alex Neil will make changes after the defeat to Birmingham City. The Lions’ head coach should revert to a 4-2-3-1 shape after the 4-4-2 formation proved unstable at St Andrew’s. Max Crocombe is currently the only true senior goalkeeper that Millwall have right now.  His poor form continued in midweek, where he prevented -1.31 goals. He will retain his place as will the rest of the back line.

Jordi Osei-Tutu is certain to play at right-back following another injury to Tristan Crama. The rest of the back four will also be the same, with a partnership of Jake Cooper and Caleb Taylor, with Zak Sturge at left-back. Millwall’s defence are averaging 1.71 goals conceded per game, and Neil will be hoping for a better performance on Saturday.

Jenson Metcalfe should come back into the starting XI after his suspension, and he will partner Derek Mazou-Sacko, who made just one accurate long ball against BCFC. Ahead of them in the advanced midfield role should be Camiel Neghli, who has been the Lions’ best performer this season. He has created 26 chances for the South London outfit and made 20 successful crosses.

Mark Sykes should come in on the right wing to bring some more stability to Millwall’s shape. The Irishman has made 29 defensive contributions and 12 clearances this campaign. On the left side, Kyrell Lisbie must retain his place to give the Lions that attacking quality. Despite his xG of 0.25 on Tuesday, the summer signing has won his side 13 fouls this term.

Up top, Neil has options to choose from with Mihailo Ivanovic and Josh Coburn back from injury. However, the Serbian was arguably rushed back against Birmingham City, and Lyndon Dykes should lead the line at The Hawthorns.

The Lions are averaging 1.29 points from their last five games, and it has seen them drop to 13th in the table and four points from the top eight. Their defeat to Birmingham City proved that Millwall need a reset to come back stronger.

Neil’s side entered the last break just a point from the play-off places, and they could enter the next one seven points away. The South London outfit had 25 points at this point last season and were sitting in 6th.

Millwall are clearly suffering from their number of injuries at the start of the campaign. Once key players like Coburn are back to full fitness, the Lions will have some catching up to do.

#2292. Alex Ferguson rievoca l'emorragia cerebrale del 2018: "Se mia moglie non fosse stata lì..."

In "Games of my life", il nuovo libro dell'iconico ex leggendario manager del Manchester United Alex Ferguson, proprio l'84enne ha ripercorso delle date simboliche nella sua carriera da allenatore. The Times ne ha pubblicato diversi estratti, ma uno in particolare ha svelato il passaggio che racconta la giornata del 5 maggio 2018, quando lo scozzese è stato vittima di un'emorragia cerebrale nella sua abitazione.

"Tutto ha iniziato a farsi buio verso le 7 del mattino. È stato in quel momento che mi sono alzato, preso da un mal di testa atroce che mi aveva impedito di dormire per quasi tutta la notte, e che mi sono subito accasciato contro una scarpiera. Ho ritrovato la luce solo 24 ore dopo, quando mi sono svegliato al Salford Royal Hospital", scrive Ferguson. "Tra il 75 e l'85% delle persone di età superiore ai 60 anni che subiscono un incidente come il mio ne muoiono", ma che per lui la salvezza è arrivata da sua moglie Cathy.

Ferguson la racconta così: "Sono sopravvissuto perché mia moglie si trovava di sotto quando ha sentito tutte le scarpe cadere rimbombando dalla scarpiera sul parquet del piano superiore. Se non fosse stata lì, l'Arbitro del Cielo avrebbe forse fischiato la fine della partita per me". La convalescenza è stata lunga, probante ma sana, fino al suo tanto atteso ritorno tra gli spalti di Old Trafford, il 22 settembre.

"L'accoglienza che mi hanno riservato gli spettatori a Old Trafford quel giorno ha contato per me più di qualsiasi cosa io abbia mai vissuto, più di quando ero ad Aberdeen dopo la nostra vittoria in Coppa delle Coppe, più delle diverse sfilate sotto i coriandoli quando allenavo l'United, più ancora che alla fine dell'ultima partita della mia carriera", ha detto Sir Alex. "Avevo la sensazione di essere accolto come se fossi tornato dal mondo dei morti. Un momento straordinario".

#2291. RILEGGI LIVE – Gattuso: “Napoli mi è rimasta nel cuore, ho vinto un trofeo. Allegri? Mai fidarsi, non ho mai vinto contro di lui”

L’allenatore della Lazio Gennaro Gattuso parlerà in questi minuti in conferenza stampa alla vigilia del match di campionato contro il Napoli. Segui con noi la diretta testuale su IamNaples.it.

“Troppe pressioni? Domani non le abbiamo, affrontiamo una squadra forte in uno stadio difficile, anche se ora in difficoltà. Sono giocatori che ha vinto sia con il Napoli che all’estero, sarà un match tosto. Bisogna pensare gara dopo gara e mancano 12 punti al primo obiettivo stagionale. A quota 40 faremo una festa, abbiamo una strada e bisogna leggere il meno possibile. Se dovessimo cadere si tornerà a lavorare come prima”.

Sull’atteggiamento della squadra: “Voglio spensieratezza e rispetto per l’avversario, i giocatori che il Napoli ha fuori fa capire il grande livello della rosa, a Oporto hanno tenuto il campo per 70 minuti poi sono mancati i cambi. Non abbiamo nulla da perdere con la nostra identità e spensieratezza, Allegri è stato il mio capitano e il mio allenatore, non ho mai vinto contro di lui e spero di fare un quarto della sua carriera. Di certo lui non muore di stress, nei momenti difficili trova sempre le cose giuste, non mi fido. Capisco che dice sarà una finale, ma anche per noi ogni gara è una finale”.

Sulla formazione: “Nuno Tavares oggi ha fatto tutto, ho comunque qualche dubbio, leggo 6 settimane per Zaccagni è una perdita importante e l’edema al quadricipite c’è. Gudmundsson? Niente Nazionale per lui, abbiamo deciso cosi, faremo un amichevole dove giocherà 40 minuti e vedremo come sta. Rovella e Marusic domani saranno in gruppo”.

Sull’esperienza in passato a Napoli, Gattuso ha commentato: “Sono stato benissimo a Napoli, ho vinto un trofeo. Il rammarico è aver avuto poco tempo i tifosi allo stadio, visto il Covid. Napoli mi è rimasta nel cuore, è stato un onore allenare quel club e sento di aver lasciato un buonissimo ricordo”.

Su Zaccagni: “Mangia con noi e domattina partirà con noi, verrà a prendere gli applausi dei tifosi alla stazione. L’ha voluto lui, fa capire lo spirito della squadra. Marusic? Può prendere minutaggio, ma lui ha giudizio e si conosce bene, ha 34 anni e sa cosa può dare. Non sono preoccupato dalla sosta, durante questi giorni affronteremo un amichevole con un club di Malta”.

Sulla sfida con il Napoli: “Non sappiamo bene come giocheranno, loro cambiano tanto. Possono giocare a 3 o a 4, vedremo. Noi dobbiamo pensare a noi stessi, la mentalità e l’atteggiamento deve essere sempre lo stesso”.

#2290. York City vs Northampton Town - prediction, team news, lineups

By Matthew Cooper | 05 Nov 2026 12:51

York City will be looking to get back to winning ways when they welcome Northampton Town to the LNER Community Stadium on Saturday for an FA Cup first round clash.

The hosts currently sit seventh in the League Two table, while the visitors are 21st.

York are looking to reach the second round of the FA Cup for just the second time since 2010, when they beat Rotherham United and Darlington before losing to Bolton Wanderers in the third round.

The Minstermen were a National League side back then, but they have since earned promotion to the EFL, winning the National League last season, and they currently occupy a League Two playoff spot.

However, they head into Saturday's cup tie after suffering successive away defeats against Barnet and Grimsby Town.

Scott Lindsey's side lost 3-1 at Barnet before suffering an even more damaging 5-2 loss at Grimsby four days later, despite initially taking the lead through Alex Hunt and Jack Hunt.

As a result, York will be looking to produce a much-improved performance against Northampton and end their losing run.

Northampton, meanwhile, were relegated from League One last season and have made a poor start to life back in League Two.

The Cobblers currently sit just two points above the relegation zone and will be hoping an FA Cup run can provide a much-needed boost.

Northampton have failed to get past the first round of the competition for the last six years and Chris Hogg's side head into Saturday's tie off the back of a 1-0 defeat to Oldham Athletic on Tuesday.

However, it is worth noting that Northampton have not lost to York in their last five meetings across all competitions, including a 1-1 draw at the LNER Community Stadium last month.

Northampton Town form (all competitions):

York could make some changes after their heavy loss to Grimsby last weekend, with Alex Newby in line to replace Joe Grey.

Newby is York's joint top scorer alongside Ollie Pearce with six goals, but he has not found the back of the net in his last seven appearances and will be keen to rediscover some form.

Northampton will be hopeful Sam Hoskins's hamstring injury is not too serious, but it appears unlikely he will be able to feature against York.

As a result, Matty Warhurst is expected to lead the line, with support from Owen Dale and Joe O'Brien-Whitmarsh.

McNally; Fallowfield, Johnson, Kitching; J. Hunt, A. Hunt, Read, Boyes; Newby, Pearce; Stones

Northampton Town possible starting lineup:

Jeacock; Donacien, Moore, Guthrie; Wormleighton, L. Evans, C. Evans, Powell; Dale, O'Brien-Whitmarsh; Warhurst

Despite suffering two disappointing defeats in recent weeks, York have made a good start to the season and should have enough to beat a struggling Northampton.

#2289. Malaga vs Racing de Santander - prediction, team news, lineups

By Matt Law | 05 Nov 2026 12:55, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 12:57

Malaga's search for their first victory of the 2026-27 La Liga campaign will continue on Saturday afternoon when they welcome Racing de Santander to Estadio La Rosaleda.

The hosts will enter the match off the back of a 2-1 defeat to Real Betis, while Racing suffered a 3-0 home loss to Real Madrid last time out.

Malaga boast a record of five draws and six defeats from their 11 matches in Spain's top flight this season, with five points leaving them at the bottom of the La Liga table.

The White and Sky Blues did record a 3-0 victory over Alcazar in the Copa del Rey on October 27, but they were unable to back that up, losing 2-1 against Real Betis in their last league fixture.

Juan Francisco Funes' side are already five points from the safety of 17th spot and will certainly be wary of that gap widening ahead of the November international break.

Malaga have faced Racing on 30 previous occasions in all competitions and lead the head-to-head, having posted 13 victories to the latter's nine.

That said, Malaga are winless in their last six matches against Racing, with their last success in this fixture coming back in February 2020 in the Segunda Division.

Racing picked up a point on their last visit to Malaga - in last season's Segunda Division campaign - while they have been victorious in three of the last five games between the two sides, which is a record that will hand them confidence.

The Green and Whites were beaten 3-0 by Real Madrid last time out, with the result leaving them 16th in the La Liga table, picking up 11 points from 11 matches courtesy of a record of three wins, two draws and six defeats.

Jose Alberto's team actually have the worst defensive record in Spain's top flight this season, conceding 29 times in their 11 games, which is eight more than Malaga.

That said, Racing have been relatively strong down the other end, scoring 15 goals, while Malaga have only found the back of the net on eight occasions.

These are the type of matches that could have a big say in determining whether Malaga, Racing or both teams are capable of avoiding relegation this season.

Racing de Santander form (all competitions):

Malaga can certainly take plenty of positives from their performance against Real Betis last time out, so it would not be a surprise to see the same XI take to the field.

Adrian Nino has scored five times in seven league appearances for Malaga this season and will once again feature in the final third of the field for the hosts.

However, all of Juan Cruz, Angel Recio, Diego Murillo, Pablo Martinez, Joaquin Munoz, Julen Lopete and Aaron Ochoa are set to miss the match due to fitness issues.

As for Racing, Jorge Salinas will make a welcome return to the squad, having served his suspension against Real Madrid last time out.

However, Andres Martin is once again expected to miss out with a muscular issue; Alvaro Mantilla is also set to be absent with the injury that forced him off early against Real Madrid.

Yassir Zabiri has scored eight times in 11 league appearances this season, and the 21-year-old will continue in the final third of the field.

Herrero; Puga, Calero, Galilea, Rafita; Enriquez; Larrubia, Dotor, Lorenzo, Aznou; Nino

Racing de Santander possible starting lineup:

Agirrezabala; Felipe, Ramon, Belocian, Salinas; I Martin, Prati; Luque, Canales, Vicente; Zabiri

Malaga's winless run will end at some stage, but we are not expecting it to happen this weekend; Racing are capable of picking up a point on the road in this fixture.

#2288. Plymouth Argyle must eye January transfer move for 10-goal EFL striker: Opinion

Plymouth Argyle will get the opportunity to freshen up their ranks this winter with some reinforcements. PAFC are sat in 5th place in the League One table after their first 14 fixtures as they eye promotion back to the Championship.

The Pilgrims should target a move for Walsall striker Aaron Pressley to add more competition and depth to their attacking department. At the age of 25, he has already picked up a decent amount of EFL experience and he has potential to grow and develop further down the line.

Pressley, who is an ex-Scotland youth international, is a key player for the Saddlers and has proved in League Two that he knows where the back of the net is. He has made 15 appearances in all competitions for Lee Grant‘s side in this campaign, 12 of which have come in the league, and he has scored 10 goals already.

Plymouth Argyle are in the play-offs along with Oxford United, Luton Town and Reading as they chase down Huddersfield Town and Stockport County. They take on non-league Alvechurch at home in the FA Cup this weekend.

Plymouth Argyle sold key attacker Lorent Tolaj to Bristol City in the summer. PAFC have since missed his presence and goal scoring touch.

Welshman Will Evans was brought in as his replacement from Mansfield Town. However, he has only scored three times and hasn’t been prolific in Devon.

The Pilgrims are the lowest scorers in the top six and need to be more ruthless in front of goal. Therefore, signing another forward in January has to be a priority.

Pressley fits the bill for Plymouth Argyle and will be full of confidence at the moment. He is under contract at Walsall until June 2028 meaning he still has just under two years left to run down.

Standing at 6ft 4inc, Pressley is solid in the air and would give Plymouth Argyle another dimension going forward. He would be useful for PAFC to throw on if they needed a goal.

The Edinburgh-born man moved down to England from Hearts as a youngster and had spells at Aston Villa and Brentford in the Premier League. He has since had stints at AFC Wimbledon, Accrington Stanley, Stevenage and Barrow.

His move to Walsall in 2025 has worked out well for all parties and he is one of their most prized assets. The Saddlers could lose him soon though and it wouldn’t be a surprise to see other teams sniffing around him.

#2286. Bradford City vs Marine AFC - prediction, team news, lineups

By Calum Burrowes | 05 Nov 2026 13:10, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 13:18

Aiming to cause a major FA Cup upset this weekend, Marine AFC travel from Merseyside to Yorkshire to face League One side Bradford City on Saturday afternoon.

The Bantams head into the cup tie without a league win in their last few outings and on the back of a weekend defeat to local rivals Huddersfield Town, while the Mariners are also winless in three matches and have slipped to 18th in the National League North table.

Bradford City will be looking to reach the second round of the FA Cup for the first time in two years when they welcome opponents from three divisions below them.

The Bantams, now led by Michael Duff following Graham Alexander's departure to Preston North End, have enjoyed some memorable cup runs over the past decade, with their journey to the quarter-finals in the 2014-15 season a notable highlight.

In January 2015, Bradford travelled to Stamford Bridge to face Jose Mourinho's Chelsea, producing one of the biggest upsets in FA Cup history as they came from two goals down after 38 minutes to secure a remarkable 4-2 victory over a side competing in the Champions League at the same time.

At the time, Chelsea were top of the Premier League while Bradford were sixth in League One, leaving 49 places between them, 20 fewer than the gap separating the Bantams from their upcoming opponents.

More recently, Bradford are still in League One, although just one win in their last five league matches has seen them slip out of the playoff places and down to seventh, seven points behind the division's leaders.

As for Marine, they make the trip to Valley Parade without a win in their last three matches, having followed a draw against Oxford City with defeats to Chester and Buxton in the National League North.

Indeed, Bobby Grant's side last tasted victory in the FA Cup, when a 2-0 win over Hartlepool United secured their place in the first round proper.

Like their upcoming opponents, the Mariners have enjoyed a famous FA Cup occasion of their own, with their 2021 meeting with Tottenham Hotspur at Rossett Park remaining a standout moment in the club's history.

The third-round tie, also against a Mourinho side, took place during the height of the COVID-19 pandemic and ended in a 5-0 victory for Spurs, with Carlos Vinicius scoring a 13-minute hat-trick.

The 161-place gap between the clubs was the largest in FA Cup history, while the sale of 32,202 "virtual tickets" raised more than £300,000 for Marine.

Since that memorable occasion, the Mariners have spent three consecutive campaigns in the National League North, where they have won just five of their opening 14 matches this season and sit 18th, only one point clear of the relegation zone.

Duff was always likely to make changes for a cup tie against opposition three divisions below Bradford, but injuries sustained in the defeat to Huddersfield could limit his options.

Adam Phillips was introduced at half-time in that match but was forced off four minutes from full-time after picking up an injury, making him unlikely to feature on Saturday.

Phillips will join Stephen Humphrys and Josh Neufville in the treatment room.

Captain Antoni Sarcevic was sent off during the Yorkshire derby last weekend, but he is available for the cup tie as his suspension only applies to league fixtures.

As for the visitors, Grant could name the same starting XI that lost 4-2 to Buxton on Halloween.

Goalkeeper Jack McIntyre has made 11 appearances this season and kept two clean sheets, and he is expected to start between the posts at Valley Parade.

Finlay Sinclair-Smith has provided four assists and scored twice in the National League North this season, and he could feature in attack alongside Charlie Jolley.

#2285. Vaclav Cerny nightmare as Celtic mission flops and Rangers hero lands transfer fear

Vaclav Cerny returned to Glasgow after Skooshgate, but it was the former Rangers hero who was left as the butt of the joke after failing to deliver against Celtic.

The Czech winger was back at Parkhead with Besiktas and tasked with tormenting the Hoops just as he had during his impressive season-long stint at Ibrox.

Martin O’Neill’s men roared to a 3-2 victory as a Camilo Duran double and Callum Osmand strike, just seconds after the youngster entered the pitch, turned the night on its head.

And these are increasingly uncertain times for Cerny.

Read more: Rangers star subject of ‘stupid’ transfer admission by SPFL rivals

The 29-year-old has never fully convinced in black and white and, hours before kick-off, transfer murmurs were already circulating in Turkey over his future. Besiktas boss Vincenzo Italiano was reportedly said to have identified Kosovo star Edon Zhegrova as an ideal replacement should Cerny depart.

The former Rangers favourite had even been publicly challenged to recapture his Old Firm magic before facing Celtic again.

Turkish journalist Ali Ece declared: “Cerny should say, ‘I’m back, this match is mine’.

“He was delivering outstanding performances in Rangers-Celtic matches.

“Cerny was the name who tormented Celtic.”

But the big comeback never materialised. Cerny failed to make the telling impact demanded of him and was hooked in the 83rd minute as Celtic closed in on victory.

Rangers fans were desperate for Cerny’s loan to become permanent in 2025, but few will have been pining for him after this return to Glasgow’s east end.

With just one goal since August, the brutal reality is that Cerny now faces a tough road to win over a demanding Besiktas support.

#2283. St Johnstone vs Dundee - prediction, team news, lineups

By Ellis Stevens | 05 Nov 2026 13:21

St Johnstone will be looking to end their winless run when they welcome Dundee to McDiarmid Park for a matchday 13 clash in the Scottish Premiership on Saturday.

The hosts are seventh in the standings with 14 points from 12 games, while the visitors are fifth with 17 points after 11 league matches.

St Johnstone have enjoyed an encouraging start to their first season back in the Scottish Premiership following their Championship-winning campaign last season, which earned them an immediate promotion back to the top division.

Simo Valakari's side have won three, drawn five and lost four of their 12 fixtures to date, leaving them seventh in the standings with 14 points, five points above the bottom two and only two points behind the top six.

The Saints are, however, enduring their longest winless run of the league campaign heading into this match, with St Johnstone failing to emerge victorious from any of their last four Scottish Premiership games, recording three draws and one defeat.

St Johnstone's biggest issue during that run has been their vulnerability in defence, with nine of the 19 goals they have conceded in the league this season coming across those four matches, including a disappointing 4-0 defeat to Rangers.

However, the Saints have also demonstrated a superb mentality and resilience, coming from behind to secure 2-2 draws with both Motherwell and Kilmarnock in their last two matches, including scoring twice with 10-men against Killie last time out.

Eager to build on those results and avoid recording a fifth straight winless match, St Johnstone will be looking to secure their first home win since beating Hibernian 2-1 at the beginning of September, as well as aiming to defeat Dundee for the first time since October 2024, when they won 2-1 on the road.

Dundee, meanwhile, head into Saturday's fixture off the back of a confidence-boosting win against Kilmarnock, with Ashley Hay scoring twice in the final 10 minutes to secure the 3-1 victory for Steven Pressley's side.

That victory lifted Dundee to fourth in the Scottish Premiership table with 17 points from 11 games, although they have since fallen behind Falkirk, following the Bairns' 2-0 victory against Celtic on matchday 12.

Dundee's matchday 12 clash with Hibernian was postponed due to Hibs' involvement in the Scottish League Cup semi-finals, meaning the Dee have also been afforded three days extra rest than their hosts.

Despite Dundee's superb start to the season, Pressley's side have face difficulties on the road, winning two and losing three of their five away fixtures in the Scottish Premiership.

However, the Dee will be able to draw confidence from their formidable recent record at McDiarmid Park, having emerged victorious from each of their last three visits to St Johnstone's home ground, while they are also unbeaten in the last five away clashes with the Saints.

St Johnstone will remain without Sam Stanton and Zach Mitchell due to ongoing injury problems, while Tom Steward is suspended after his 13th-minute red card against Kilmarnock on matchday 12.

Steward should be replaced by Ross Sinclair between the posts, with an unchanged back three of Cheick Diabate, Bozo Mikulic and Enes Alic likely to feature.

Meanwhile, Bobby Wales, Finlay Robertson and Simon Murray are all unavailable due to injury issues, while the remainder of the squad should be fit and fully rested after Dundee's matchday 12 match was postponed.

Hay scored two late goals to seal the victory for Dundee in their last outing, and the striker should continue alongside Bradley Fink, with Joe Bevan and Alan Forrest providing support from the wings.

Sinclair; Diabate, Mikulic, Alic; Todd, McAlear, Holt, Cotterill; Fowler, Steven; Schmidt

Goodman; D Wright, J Wright, Astley, Samuels; Bevan, Jones, Hamilton, Forrest; Hay, Fink

St Johnstone have a lacklustre recent record against Dundee, especially at home, and head into this match off the back of their longest Scottish Premiership winless run this term.

Given Dundee have also had three days extra rest after their matchday 12 fixture was postponed, we are predicting the away side to secure all three points.

#2282. Derby County need to target winter swoop for 25-year-old after Blackburn Rovers summer interest: Opinion

Derby County are in the Championship relegation zone and have struggled for points. DCFC will get the chance to bolster their squad when the January transfer window opens up for business.

Dundee United left-back Will Ferry was heavily linked with a move to Blackburn Rovers in the summer. However, nothing materialised on that front in the end and he remained in Scotland beyond the deadline in September.

The Rams should now turn their attention to the defender and try and lure him to Pride Park this winter. Ferry, 25, is out of contract at the end of the season and his current club risk losing him for free in June 2027 if they don’t sell him in January, assuming he doesn’t pen an extension anytime soon.

The Bury-born man has made 91 appearances for Dundee United in all competitions to date, eight of which have come in this campaign, and he has chipped in with five goals and an eye-catching 15 assists from the back. He has a nice left foot and should move back down to England soon for a new challenge in his career.

Derby County currently have Craig Forsyth and Derry Murkin in the treatment room. Therefore, DCFC could do with another option in their position on the left flank of defence.

The Rams have had to play the likes of Ryan Hedges and Ryan Nyambe there over recent times. Ferry would be ideal for them now and could plug a gap for the time being.

The Republic of Ireland international, who has one cap to his name, has proved himself up in Scotland. He likes to put crosses into the box as much as possible and attacking players like Carlton Morris and Patrick Agyemang (when he’s fit) will benefit from his delivery.

Ferry rose up through Southampton’s academy before he was loaned out to Crawley Town as a youngster to get some experience under his belt. He then had two permanent years at Cheltenham Town before Dundee United came calling for his services.

Dundee United will want to receive a fee of some sort for Ferry and not lose him for nothing next year. Derby County should try and test their resolve.

The Rams can’t afford to get their recruitment wrong in January and need the right type of characters. At 25, Ferry’s next move is a big one and he needs to go somewhere he is guaranteed regular minutes.

Up next for Derby County is a clash away against Ferry’s old team Southampton after the international break. The Saints are eyeing promotion to the Premier League under Tonda Eckert.

Carlisle United will be looking to cause an upset when they welcome Salford City to Brunton Park on Saturday in the first round of the FA Cup.

The hosts currently sit eighth in the National League, while the visitors are top of League Two.

Carlisle head into Saturday's tie in excellent form, having gone unbeaten in the last seven matches across all competitions with six wins and one draw.

That run includes a 2-1 win over Tamworth to reach the first round of the FA Cup and it is also worth noting that Carlisle pulled off a major upset in last year's competition.

The Cumbrians beat League One side Reading 3-2 in the first round thanks to a dramatic late hat trick from Regan Linney as they defeated a club two divisions above them for the first time in the FA Cup.

Rob Elliot's side will be hoping lightning can strike twice this weekend and they do have a historic edge over Salford, having only lost to them twice in their nine meetings across all competitions.

Salford have enjoyed an excellent start to the new season, with their impressive record of 10 wins, one draw and three defeats leaving them five points clear at the top of League Two.

Peter Cklamovski's side are unbeaten in their last eight games and have won their last five, including a 2-1 win over Exeter City in their most recent match last weekend.

The Ammies have enjoyed strong showings in the FA Cup in recent years, reaching the third in 2025 and the fourth round in 2026 where they were beaten on both occassions by Manchester City.

Carlisle could be forced to make a change, with Teddy Jenks a doubt after picking up a knock against Altrincham.

#2278. Aberdeen vs Hibernian - prediction, team news, lineups

By Ellis Stevens | 05 Nov 2026 13:54

Aberdeen and Hibernian will meet at Pittodrie Stadium on Saturday evening for a matchday 13 clash in the Scottish Premiership.

The hosts are 10th in the standings with 11 points from 11 games, while the visitors are bottom of the table with eight points from 11 fixtures.

Aberdeen have endured a lacklustre start to the 2026-27 Scottish Premiership campaign, with Stephen Robinson's side sitting 10th in the standings after 11 league games.

The Dons have recorded only three wins and two draws during that time, alongside six defeats, leaving them narrowly just two points above the drop zone and three points above their bottom-placed visitors.

Aberdeen do have a game in hand on the three teams above them, meaning they will have the opportunity to climb up the standings if they secure a victory, while a win on Saturday could also be crucial to avoid slipping into the drop zone.

The Dons will, however, head into the matchday 13 clash short on confidence after suffering three straight defeats, with Scottish Premiership losses to Dundee United (3-2) and Falkirk (1-0) and a heartbreaking late 1-0 loss to Rangers in the Scottish League Cup semi-final last time out.

With their full focus now on improving their league form, the under-pressure Robinson will be desperate for his side to snap their three-game losing streak and claim all three points when they welcome Hibernian to Pittodrie.

Aberdeen have been at their best when playing at their home ground in the league this season, claiming seven of their 11 points from home fixtures, after two wins, one draw and three defeats.

Hibernian, however, have been at their strongest when playing on the road, accumulating seven of their eight points from away matches in the Scottish Premiership.

Hibs have won two, drawn one and lost two of their five away league fixtures, with their disastrous home form the main reason behind their current standing at the bottom of the Scottish Premiership table.

Hibernian remain the only team in the division yet to win a home match in the league this season, with one draw and five defeats from six games played at Easter Road Stadium.

That miserable home form played a large part in David Gray's dismissal in mid-September, with the club legend dismissed after losing 1-0 to Kilmarnock, which marked their sixth consecutive home league defeat.

New boss Marink Reedijk failed to make an immediate impact upon his appointment in early October, losing four and drawing one of his four league fixtures at the helm, but the new boss did finally taste victory for the first time as Hibernian manager at the weekend.

Hibernian came from behind to defeat city rivals Hearts 2-1 in the Scottish League Cup semi-final, booking their place in the final against Rangers and earning plenty of favour for struggling boss Reedijk.

The manner of the victory will be particularly pleasing, with Hibernian creating 2.89 expected goals (xG) to Hearts' 1.01xG, and Hibs, who are the Scottish Premiership's lowest goalscorers with 11 goals, will be hoping to demonstrate that attacking ability once again on Saturday.

Hibernian had the better of this fixture throughout the 2025-26 season, winning two, losing one and drawing one of their three clashes with Aberdeen, including winning 2-1 at Pittodrie in October 2025.

Aberdeen do not have any injury concerns or absences heading into Saturday's encounter, leaving Robinson with a fully available squad to choose from.

Robinson is expected to make several changes from the team that lost to Rangers in the Scottish League Cup semi-final, with the likes of Chris Cadden, Stuart Armstrong and Lewis Smith, among others, potentially in line to start.

Meanwhile, Hibernian are unable to call upon the services of Callum Wright, Nathan lowe, Jordan Obita, Kanayochukwu Megwa and Rocky Bushiri due to ongoing injury issues.

Owen Elding, Jamie McGrath and Martin Boyle all played key parts in their superb 2-1 semi-final win against Hearts last time out, and the trio should all continue their relationship in attack on Saturday evening.
