#2277. 3 players who Birmingham City should offload in January, including Lee Buchanan

Birmingham City have enjoyed a fine season to date after a slightly different approach in the summer transfer window. BCFC made seven new signings, two of whom had already been at the club, so it was a window of evolution not revolution as opposed to previous squad rebuilds.

But, with Chris Davies‘ side firmly in the Championship promotion conversation, the January window offers a chance to strengthen. The Blues can shift out some fringe players to make way for additions who can push them over the line in the bid to make the Premier League.

With that in mind, we put forward three players who should be leaving Birmingham City in the winter…

Somewhat surprisingly, Scottish winger Scott Wright stayed at St Andrew’s in the summer. The 29-year-old spent the second half of last season on loan at Dundee and the strong competition for minutes on the Birmingham City wings meant minutes were bound to be limited.

That has proven to be the case with Wright making only two Championship matchday squads and playing just one minute of senior football in a brief substitute appearance against Swansea City in the Carabao Cup.

Wright sees his contract expire in 2027, but to get back to playing regularly, he should leave BCFC in the winter. A return to Scotland or drop to League One seem like his most likely options.

Another player who has been on the fringes at Birmingham City is Lee Buchanan, although his situation is a little different. There was talk of a potential summer loan move as the left-back rebuilt his fitness after a serious knee injury, but he ended up staying put.

Alex Cochrane‘s emergence in the starting XI and the signing of Kristoffer Lund have left Buchanan as third choice in his favoured position. The Blues have been eager to be careful with his minutes given how long he was out, but by the time January comes around, he should be ready to play more regualrly.

There may be hesitance to cash in right away, so a loan feels more likely for January. Then, his situation can be reassessed in the summer, as he has shown he can be a Championship option before.

Last but not least is Marvin Ducksch, who has made eight Championship appearances off the bench. The German striker showed signs of promise in his debut campaign at Birmingham City, but a drink driving charge brought doubts over his future.

A summer move failed to materialise, but it is still widely expected that the 32-year-old moves on this season. A switch back to Germany is most likely, and could see Ducksch return to impressive form given his record of 71 goals in 138 2. Bundesliga appearances, and 34 in 130 in the Bundesliga.

August Priske is the lead striker at BCFC with Jay Stansfield and Luis Vasquez as the other options. Another centre forward may be needed if Ducksch is sold, but that seems like a logical switch.

#2276. Accrington Stanley vs Wycombe Wanderers - prediction, team news, lineups

By Calum Burrowes | 05 Nov 2026 14:11, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 20:21

Both looking to reach the second round of the FA Cup once again, League Two side Accrington Stanley welcome third-tier opponents Wycombe Wanderers to the Crown Ground on Saturday afternoon, with both clubs entering the competition at this stage.

The Reds are enjoying their best spell of the season and head into the weekend's clash on the back of a 10-game unbeaten run, while the Chairboys continued their push towards the playoff places last week with a 2-0 victory over Burton Albion.

Accrington Stanley would need to win three matches in this season's FA Cup to equal their best run in the competition, having most recently reached the fourth round during the 2022-23 campaign before being denied another opportunity to repeat that achievement in 2025 when a trip to Anfield ended in a 4-0 defeat.

John Doolan remains in charge of the Reds and will oversee his 135th match as Accrington boss on Saturday, with his side currently enjoying one of the most encouraging spells of his tenure.

The Reds endured a difficult start to the campaign, recording just one victory in their opening eight matches across all competitions, but they have since turned their fortunes around and remain unbeaten since early September.

Impressive victories over top-half opponents such as Cheltenham Town and Gillingham have helped Doolan's side climb to ninth in the League Two table and extend their unbeaten run to 10 matches across league and EFL Trophy action, although seven of those fixtures have ended level after 90 minutes.

Two of those draws came in cup competitions and were ultimately settled by penalty shootouts, but Accrington have still drawn half of their league matches this season, with no side in the English Football League recording more.

Wycombe Wanderers, meanwhile, make the 200-mile-plus journey from Buckinghamshire to Lancashire hoping to extend their unbeaten run to five matches after enjoying an encouraging start under their new manager.

Michael Duff was relieved of his duties at the beginning of September and replaced by Tom Hounsell, who initially took charge on an interim basis before being handed the job permanently following an impressive start.

Since his arrival, the Chairboys have suffered just one defeat in 10 matches across all competitions, losing 4-1 to Luton Town, while their improved results have lifted them from the relegation zone to 13th in the League One table, leaving them four points adrift of the top six.

Their most recent victory came last weekend, when goals from Jayden Wareham and Cauley Woodrow secured a comfortable 2-0 win over Burton Albion.

Hounsell has overseen a 60% win rate since taking charge, marking an impressive beginning to his time at Adams Park, and he will now prepare for his first FA Cup fixture as Wycombe look to reach the second round for the fourth consecutive season.

Wycombe Wanderers form (all competitions):

Accrington Stanley could have the option of naming an unchanged XI from the side that drew with Northampton Town at the weekend, although the availability of one of their leading goalscorers remains uncertain.

Josh Woods has found the net seven times in 12 League Two appearances this season but missed the latest fixture and could remain unavailable for Saturday's cup tie.

Louie Moulden is expected to retain his place between the posts, with Devon Matthews, Farrend Rawson and Joseph Anderson likely to continue as part of a familiar back three.

As for the visiting Chairboys, Hounsell could take the opportunity to rotate his squad and rest a few key players with a demanding run of fixtures awaiting his side over the coming weeks.

Former England and Liverpool striker Danny Ings has largely been used from the bench since joining Wycombe but could be handed a starting opportunity in the FA Cup this weekend.

Wareham, however, strengthened his case for another start with his sixth goal in just nine appearances last time out, giving Hounsell a decision to make.

Conor Hazard has been Wycombe's first-choice goalkeeper throughout the season but could make way for Joe Wildsmith, who featured in the club's most recent EFL Trophy fixture.

The Reds have shown plenty of resilience during their unbeaten run and should make life difficult for Wycombe, but we expect Hounsell's side to have enough attacking quality to secure their place in the second round.

#2273. Why Celtic vs Kilmarnock has an unusual 4pm kick-off but isn't on TV

Celtic’s clash with Kilmarnock has been handed an unusual 4pm Sunday kick-off despite the game not being selected for live TV coverage.

Martin O’Neill’s side welcome Killie to Celtic Park on Sunday, November 8, but supporters won’t be able to watch the Premiership encounter live on Sky Sports or Premier.

The later start is understood to have been agreed between all parties, including Police Scotland, because of Remembrance Sunday events and significant roadworks around Glasgow.

Police resources will be in demand earlier in the day as serving military personnel, veterans and civic leaders gather in George Square to mark Remembrance Sunday.

Read more: We were superior to Celtic but ‘a bit of chaos’ cost us the result we deserved

Glasgow’s main remembrance service returns to George Square after being temporarily moved to Glasgow Cathedral while redevelopment work affected the square and Cenotaph. The public service begins at 10.45am and is expected to finish at around 11.45am.

Travel around the city is also being affected by essential gantry refurbishment work on the M8 and M77, which is ongoing from Friday, October 30 until Saturday, November 28.

The combination of those factors means Celtic and Kilmarnock will have to wait until 4pm before getting down to business in the east end of Glasgow.

And there is recent precedent. The corresponding fixture last season, staged on the same Remembrance weekend, was also given a 4pm Sunday kick-off.

#2272. Data estimates how much Sheffield United star Sydie Peck is worth as midweek hattrick continues excellent start to the season

Sheffield United continued their impressive run of form on Tuesday night, securing a 3-2 victory away at Preston North End. SUFC are now unbeaten in four matches and have won four of their last six Championship outings.

It was an impressive all-round team display, but Sydie Peck was undoubtedly the standout performer. The 22-year-old midfielder produced a superb hat-trick in a man-of-the-match performance, becoming the first Sheffield United player to score a competitive first-team hat-trick since Billy Sharp against Aston Villa in February 2019.

Peck’s display was another reminder of his growing importance to the Blades, having also been the subject of recent exit speculation. The midfielder was linked with a move to now fellow Championship side West Ham over the summer.

Peck arrived at Bramall Lane from Arsenal’s academy as a free agent in 2021 and has made 105 appearances for the first team after a brief National League loan at Oldham Athletic.

SUFC will likely not allow Peck to go for a small fee, with a fee of £15m as a baseline the likely ball-park. Whether sides keen on the England U21 midfielder would value him at that is unknown.

FootballTransfers.com’s Estimated Transfer Value (ETV) model places him at €16.2 million (approximately £13.5m at current rates), with a range of €14.6m–€17.8m. This sits above the current FootballTransfers valuation of Divine Mukasa (around €12.8m) and is higher than most Championship players of a similar age-range.

Peck’s age as well as his status as an England youth international will see his value be higher than some other Championship midfielders.

Peck made his senior breakthrough during the 2024/25 campaign before taking another significant step forward last season, registering five goals and five assists in 41 appearances across all competitions.

The London-born midfielder attracted interest during the summer, but he made clear his desire to repay those who have shown faith in him at Bramall Lane, including manager Chris Wilder.

Peck remains happy at Sheffield United, and his performances this season suggest staying put has done little to slow his development. However, if he maintains his current level, further interest is inevitable, potentially making a move away increasingly difficult to turn down.

Sheffield United are keen to secure a new contract for Peck, with his existing agreement due to expire in 2028. The Blades would be wise to act sooner rather than later, with his recent displays likely to attract plenty of attention from elsewhere.

Dundee United had a slow start to the 2026-27 Scottish Premiership season, failing to win any of their opening three games and recording just one victory in their first six, alongside three defeats and two draws.

However, a 3-0 victory over St Mirren on matchday seven sparked a significant improvement in form, with the Tangerines subsequently recording two wins and two draws in their next four league matches, including drawing 2-2 with reigning champions Celtic last time out.

#2269. From Leao to Quenda: Five players who could inherit Cristiano Ronaldo's No.7 shirt for Portugal

By Lewis Blain | 05 Nov 2026 14:57

A few months ago, the thought seemed impossible but now, it is the reality.

For the first time in more than two decades, Portugal are preparing for an international future without the legendary Cristiano Ronaldo.

The curtain has finally come down on one of football's greatest international careers, with the five-time Ballon d'Or winner stepping away after 234 appearances, 146 goals, and a trophy haul that reshaped Selecao's place in the game.

From Euro 2016 glory to two Nations League triumphs, Ronaldo did more than lead a generation. He became the face of an entire footballing nation. And with his departure comes another symbolic passing of the torch.

The iconic number seven shirt, worn by Ronaldo for 23 years and recognised across every corner of the sport, is now vacant. The question facing Portugal is no longer how to replace an icon, but who is ready to inherit the most famous shirt in the country's football history.

Rafael Leao has already worn the number in Ronaldo's absence, but a new generation appears to be emerging for Portugal.

From established stars to wonderkids tipped for greatness, Sports Mole takes a look at the contenders waiting for their chance to carry one of the most prestigious international shirts into a new era.

Leao is perhaps the closest thing Portugal have produced to a superstar winger since Ronaldo.

When the 27-year-old is in full flight, his combination of physicality, explosive acceleration and close control makes him almost impossible to contain, giving the Selecao a frightening attacking outlet.

Now at Galatasaray after leaving AC Milan, Leao has already taken on the number seven shirt during Ronaldo's recent absence. He enjoyed three consecutive seasons of double-digit goal contributions in Milan, although his international return still has room for improvement, with six goals and eight assists in 51 caps for Portugal.

There is little doubt that Leao has the ability to become Portugal's leading attacking figure, but inheriting Ronaldo's shirt brings expectations that go beyond individual brilliance.

He must deliver more consistently on the international stage.

Felix has endured an inconsistent club career since his record-breaking move to Atletico Madrid in 2019, but he remains one of Portugal's most technically gifted attacking players.

The Al Nassr forward, now only a club teammate of Ronaldo, offers something different to Leao. Rather than operating as a traditional centre-forward, he is at his best drifting between the lines as a creative shadow striker, using his movement and close control to unlock stubborn defences.

His international form has also been encouraging, with 21 goal contributions in 61 appearances for Portugal. And with Ronaldo no longer occupying the central attacking role, Felix could benefit from increased responsibility in a more fluid forward line.

Neto wears number 18, which already contains a subtle nod to seven, if you're willing to do the maths. His explosive pace and direct style make him a compelling contender for the role.

Injuries have regularly interrupted his progress, yet the Chelsea attacker remains a dangerous option when fully fit. Blues boss Xabi Alonso has also used him in a wing-back role this season, although his influence in the final third has not disappeared, with four goal contributions in nine Premier League appearances.

Neto may not possess the same natural goalscoring instincts as Ronaldo, but his directness, crossing and ability to carry the ball at speed would give Portugal a different type of attacking threat.

If he can maintain his fitness and improve his finishing, he could force his way into the conversation.

Much like Ronaldo, Quenda began his senior career at Sporting CP before earning a £40m move to Chelsea, with the teenager now hoping to follow in the footsteps of his illustrious predecessor.

The winger has been described by former youth coach Fabio Roque as a player with explosive qualities, unpredictability and creativity, even drawing comparisons with Arsenal's Bukayo Saka.

Quenda's performances for Portugal's youth sides have also attracted attention, with his pace and fearless approach marking him out as one of the country's most exciting prospects.

He is still waiting for his senior international debut and has had limited opportunities since joining Chelsea. However, his pace, bravery in possession and ability to operate on either flank make him an exciting long-term candidate to inherit the famous shirt.

Mora is another teenager who could eventually become the creative heartbeat of Portugal, having already demonstrated remarkable technical maturity for someone so young.

The attacking midfielder progressed through Porto's academy and became the club's youngest professional player at 15, before making his senior debut in 2024. After helping Porto win the Portuguese league in 2026, he joined AS Roma in August in a deal worth an initial £20m, alongside a reported £100m release clause.

Mora is particularly impressive in tight spaces, using his vision and quick decision-making to find passes before defensive lines have time to react. He has already begun gaining experience at senior level but is yet to make his debut for Portugal, despite previously receiving a call-up and being part of the 2025 Nations League-winning squad.

At 19, there is no need to rush him into the senior side, but his ceiling is enormous. If he fulfils his potential, Mora could become one of the defining players of Portuguese football over the next decade.

Ronaldo's number seven will never be an easy shirt to inherit, and Portugal may not immediately settle on one permanent successor.

Leao is the most obvious present-day candidate, with Felix and Neto offering established alternatives, but Quenda and Mora could ultimately represent the country's most exciting long-term possibilities.

#2268. 3 midfielders Blackburn Rovers should target in January after Mowbray admission, including 2018/19 loan star

Blackburn Rovers only signed three players in the summer transfer window, and sights are already set on January targets. Tony Mowbray has voiced a need to bolster in central midfield, with Adam Forshaw’s absence leaving only Kristi Montgomery, Sam Morsy, Moussa Baradji and Jake Garrett to pick from.

Rovers aren’t flush for cash, so they will likely have to be shrewd in their winter business. A lack of experience in the absences of Morsy and Forshaw mean some extra pedigree would be welcomed too.

Here, we put forward three options for the recruitment team at Ewood Park to consider…

First up is someone who would be a highly popular signing at Blackburn Rovers. Midfielder Harrison Reed spent a highly successful loan spell at Ewood Park in the 2018/19 season, providing three goals and seven assists in 36 games while away from former club Southampton.

He would join Fulham off the back of his Rovers stay, and he has played 212 times for the club since. However, Reed is down the pecking order under Alvaro Arbeloa having failed to make a Premier League matchday squad since August, despite being fit and available.

A drop to the Championship would give him the game time he deserves. Reed would bring experience, leadership and a well-rounded skillset to Tony Mowbray’s side and would be a very impressive addition.

A player in a similar position to Reed is Alan Browne, who has bags of Championship experience to his name but is currently struggling for minutes in the Premier League with Sunderland. The Irishman has only played in cup competitions and it seems highly unlikely that he makes a resurgence at the Stadium of Light.

Browne has been a player of interest to Blackburn Rovers before, and his vast second tier experience would make him an influential player on and off the pitch. The 31-year-old would bring leadership in the dressing room, offering an energetic box-to-box presence in midfield.

This paired with a strong technical game and versatility made Browne a regular in Middlesbrough’s push for the Championship play-offs while on loan last season.

Last but not least is a more left-field option, but Julius Magnusson would be a great player for Blackburn Rovers and potentially a real hit in the Championship. The 28-year-old Iceland international is a key man for Swedish side Elfsborg, and he has stood out over their 2026 season.

Magnusson is a very well-rounded player, chipping in with six goals and three assists in 30 games across all competitions this year, as per Fotmob. Amongst Allsvenskan midfielders, he ranks amongst the best in defensive contributions (200 – top 1%), interceptions (53 – top 3%), aerial duels won (55 – top 1%), line-breaking passes (217 – top 13%) and touches (1,659 – top 8%).

Those stats reflect his wide skillset, and at his age, he likely wouldn’t break the bank. Magnusson could come in and be a real hit for Rovers if they are happy to pay a fee to pry him out of his contract until 2029.

St Mirren welcome high-flying Rangers to the Marbill Coaches Stadium on Saturday for a matchday 13 clash in the Scottish Premiership.

They secured an incredible 3-2 win this week against Ararat Armenia with two injury time goals despite having to play for more than an hour with ten men.

They welcome the Hoops next and Nuno is determined to continue to make Portugal proud.

He said: "That was an unforgettable night for everyone at Torreense, for the people at the club. "Given how the season has gone, it was an important win for the fans. Everyone identified with what we are trying to do.

"It was an absolutely epic victory and I could not ask for a better debut as a manager.

"Celtic are next here, so we will fight to do everything to try to get points and try to win the game.

"Let's continue our dream because we have done beautiful things in Europe this season.

"We want to continue fighting to dignify Portuguese football and I think today we lived up to its standards and the Portuguese people can be proud of us."

By Matthew Cooper | 05 Nov 2026 15:03

Peterborough United will be looking to get back to winning ways when they welcome Reading to the Weston Homes Stadium on Saturday in the first round of the FA Cup.

The hosts currently sit 22nd in the League One table, while the visitors are sixth.

Peterborough are without a win in their last five matches across all competitions, having struggled for form after winning their first two games under new head coach Robbie Savage.

The Posh lost 2-0 at home to Luton in their most recent fixture last weekend, leaving them third from bottom and two points from safety.

Savage was critical of his side's performance against Luton, admitting that "if you can’t defend in the opening moments of halves or take your chances, you won’t win games of football".

Peterborough will be looking to put in a much-improved performance in the FA Cup this weekend and they will be buoyed by the fact they went unbeaten against Reading last season, with one win and a draw.

Reading, meanwhile, will be looking for a response after they lost 2-0 to local rivals Oxford United on Wednesday.

Richardson has backed the Royals to "come again" when they travel to Peterborough on Saturday and he will be keen to see some real improvements.

Peterborough United form (all competitions):

#2264. Data estimates how much Cardiff City star Jack Moylan is worth ahead of potential January interest

Cardiff City have made a respectable start to life back in the Championship, although Brian Barry-Murphy’s side have struggled to find consistency in front of goal. Their problems in the final third have arguably left them lower down the table than their overall performances deserve.

However, those struggles have not affected summer recruit Jack Moylan. The Bluebirds decision to bring the attacking midfielder in from Lincoln City is already looking like an excellent piece of business.

The 25-year-old has quickly established himself as one of the Bluebirds’ most influential players, registering nine goals and three assists in his opening 14 Championship appearances. Moylan has also started every league fixture, highlighting just how important he has become to Barry-Murphy’s plans.

With Cardiff struggling towards the lower end of the table, his contributions have been particularly valuable. If he continues producing at this level, the Bluebirds may have to contend with growing interest in their summer signing when the January transfer window opens.

While the fee was reported as undisclosed upon Moylan’s announcement, reports have suggested that Cardiff City was an initial £3m which could rise to £5m.

The Bluebirds have tied Moylan down to a four-year deal running until the summer of 2028. With that in mind, if interest does arrive in January, they are in a strong position to ask for a hefty fee and profit.

However, the data suggests that Cardiff City may have overpaid to bring Moylan in this summer, which does not bode well for a potential sale. FootballTransfers’ estimated transfer value has the attacking-midfielder’s value at €2.2m, which is around £1.8m.

Off the current Cardiff City squad, Moylan has the second highest value with only Yousef Salech higher (€3.8m). Moylan’s value is also considerably lower than the only player who has more Championship goals then him Will Lankshear, who is valued at €20.7m.

A more direct comparison may be Blackburn Rovers striker Andri Gudjohnsen, who also has nine goals this campaign. His value is only €2.7m and much similar to Moylan’s, while being just a year younger.

Moylan’s statistics underline just how influential he has become for Cardiff City. His attacking numbers are particularly impressive, with 51 shots, 19 on target and 18 chances created.

He has also registered five big chances created, while his nine goals have come from an expected goals figure of 5.48, highlighting his clinical finishing and ability to outperform the underlying numbers.

The Irishman has delivered in some of Cardiff’s biggest performances, scoring twice in their 5-1 victory over Watford while impressing in a central striking role. He also scored a first-half hat-trick in the 3-3 draw with West Ham during the week.

Having progressed from Irish football to becoming a key figure in Lincoln’s League One title-winning campaign, Moylan has adapted impressively to the Championship.

First, Ignazio Abate's side conceded three late goals at home to Como, before Frosinone struck twice in the second half last weekend.

Either home or away, Lecce have lost five of the last eight Serie A meetings, failing to even score in six.

The matchwinner in this fixture last season, Che Adams is set to partner Giovanni Simeone up front; since his arrival in 2024, the Scotland striker is Toro's top Serie A scorer with 19 goals.

Highlighting their dearth of potent forwards, Lecce's last eight league goals have been scored by as many different players - including midfielder Youssef Maleh last week.

Willem Geubbels and Lassana Coulibaly will both be assessed before Saturday's game, but Joel Monteiro injured his left calf against Genoa; Medon Berisha, Santiago Pierotti and Ivan Ilic are still sidelined.

Bolstering his options at the back, Di Francesco can recall Kialonda Gaspar, who returns after serving a two-match suspension.

#2260. 19-year-old Southampton starlet showcasing exciting potential in European loan after early transfer interest

Southampton sent eight players out on loan this campaign. With a mixture of academy talents and players who are on their way out of St Mary’s, the Saints are seeing mixed success from the departed members of the squad.

Moses Sesay, Nicholas Oyekunle and Jay Robinson were the three teenagers to leave for the season. It’s the latter who Tonda Eckert will be monitoring closely after a breakout term last year in the Championship.

Robinson made 28 appearances for Southampton, scoring twice and providing an assist. Minutes were always going to be hard to come by for the 19-year-old this season. With a move to Monza secured, the attacker has plenty to prove in the Serie A.

Securing a loan move to Serie A is a positive sign of how much potential Robinson has to unlock. It’s safe to say that despite a slow start to life at Monza, the 19-year-old is becoming a key player for the Italian outfit.

The Southampton academy graduate had to wait until September to make his first start, and he impressed in a 1-0 win against Torino. Featuring in an attacking midfield role, he completed the most dribbles in the game with four and had nine touches inside the opposition’s box.

His first goal came in the next match, in which he completed 78% of his passes and was dispossessed only once. Former Saints boss Ivan Juric has shown full faith in Robinson, starting the teenager in three of the last four outings.

The 19-year-old’s best display came against Cagliari, where he scored once in a 4-0 win. Two out of his three dribbles were successful in the game, and he made three line-breaking passes. It has been an impressive loan stint for the youngster with Inter Milan registering their interest.

Southampton have the option to recall Robinson from his loan spell in January. However, given his promising spell so far, the Saints would be reckless to do so.

At left-wing, the South Coast outfit currently have the options of Leo Scienza and Sam Edozie, as well as Lewis Dobbin, who can play there too. Edozie is likely to depart in January, though, after putting in a transfer request late in the summer window.

This would ultimately leave Eckert’s side short of attackers in that position. However, they must avoid recalling Robinson to improve their depth. Even if Edozie were to leave, his exit would give Dobbin a better chance of breaking into the first team after just 409 minutes played.

Most importantly, it would do no favours to the 19-year-old’s development. The Southampton prospect has only recently become settled at Monza, and with his minutes increasing over the last couple of months, the South Coast outfit can’t afford to ruin his crucial playing time.

Deportivo have not been victorious away to Espanyol since June 2007, meanwhile, which is an indication of the size of their task in this contest.

The Blue and Whites, who finished second in last season's Segunda Division to secure a return to the top flight, are currently eighth in the La Liga table, boasting a record of three wins, five draws and three defeats to collect 14 points.

Aubameyang has scored five goals in eight league appearances this season but is again expected to feature as a substitute rather than being introduced into the starting XI given that Bil Nsongo has two goals in his last three appearances.

Adama Traore is again expected to feature off the bench for the visitors this weekend after impressing in the second half of the Osasuna fixture.

#2257. Huddersfield Town must demand £7m-£8m for 'unplayable' star better than Sorba Thomas

Huddersfield Town are flying at the top of League One right now, and if the Terriers can maintain this level of performance and form throughout the course of the campaign, the Championship beckons, though one player will be key to those efforts.

Huddersfield sit at the top of League One right now with eight wins, five draws, and just one defeat in 14 league games thus far, as Martin Drury has his side playing some excellent stuff.

The likes of Archie Collins, Ibane Bowat, and Ilias Bronkhorst headed to the ACCU Stadium over the summer, and following Bronkhorst from Excelsior to West Yorkshire was Dutch winger Derensili Sanches Fernandes, who has captured the attention of the entire EFL with his scintillating performances so far.

We rank all 24 League One stadiums by their ground's distance from the city or town, starting with the longest distance.

Sanches Fernandes, 25, has quickly become Huddersfield's star man up top, as alongside Welsh forward Joe Taylor, the winger is absolutely thriving right now, with seven goals and six assists in just 14 league games so far.

Sanches Fernandes trails only MK Dons' Aaron Collins in the combined goals and assists numbers in League One right now, but it would be no surprise to see the 25-year-old overtake the former Bolton Wanderers striker before too long.

The Dutchman, of Cape Verdean descent, has already played down exit talk following his excellent performances, telling Dutch media outlet Voetbal: "It's all happening way too fast. I also read those crazy things, for example, that I'll be playing in the Premier League as early as winter.

"I'm not thinking about that at all right now; whatever happens, happens, but I'm going to fight to bring Huddersfield back to the level where they belong. His dream of playing in the Premier League himself is very much alive. I am still two steps away."

Football League World's Huddersfield fan pundit, Graeme Rayner, has shared their thoughts on what Sanches Fernandes' asking fee should be right now amid an incredible start to life in West Yorkshire.

Graeme said: "Derensili Sanches Fernandes, until the summer, I would wager that pretty much no Huddersfield Town fan, except perhaps the Football Manager computer game geeks, had heard of him, and even those might not have.

"He came out of nowhere in terms of a purchase for us, and the scouting that's been involved has clearly been fantastic, because both he and Ilias Bronkhorst link really well on the right-hand side, and he has been the standout player in League One this season.

"He's been phenomenal. I think he's scored seven and got six assists, I believe it is. So he's got a goal involvement pretty much every game. That's 13 goal involvements in 14 League One games.

"He is quite often pivotal in build-up play to goals where he doesn't get the assist as well. His use of the bullish phenomenon, his ability to run at players is great; his touch is great, he's strong, he's quick. At times, he has looked unplayable at League One level. And having watched a lot of Championship football over recent years, I would say that he'd probably be very effective in the Championship as well.

"In terms of his price tag, the good news is Huddersfield Town don't need to sell, and he's already said that he wants to stay. So the good news is that in January, if someone wants to come in, they're going to have to pay a lot of money. I would say his market value, therefore, is probably somewhat inflated.

"Sorba Thomas was the last right-winger we had at the club who was involved in as many goals as he is. In the summer, I believe he went for around £7 million, with potential add-ons, for a £10 million transfer to Hull City. A lot of Town fans already, and we have to bear in mind he's playing League One, think he's better than Sorba Thomas and his ceiling might be higher.

"But I would say if anybody comes in with an offer of less than £6 million-£7 million in January, it wouldn't even be considered. Obviously, every player has their price. If someone comes in with £7 million-£8 million, maybe we'd accept it. I don't know. But I would say it's going to be silly money. It's got to be silly money because it's more important to us.

"The value to us is higher by getting promoted than selling him. So that's what I would say. I just don't think that we need to sell him."

What have you made of Sanches Fernandes so far? What would you value him at? Does he have a higher ceiling than Sorba Thomas? Share your thoughts below!

Naturally, if a player is being linked with a move from League One to the Premier League, it takes great character to cancel that outside noise and remain focused purely on the current situation.

As for Sanches Fernandes, he appears to be doing just that, and if he does indeed commit the entire campaign to Huddersfield and remains fit, the Terriers are surely a Championship club in waiting.

Under contract until the summer of 2030 and showing no signs of forcing an exit, Sanches Fernandes would command a lucrative fee for Huddersfield, and no matter how much their resolve is tested, it will surely take a huge number to prise him away from West Yorkshire anytime soon.

Collect every point to win the round. Don’t let Neil Warnock catch you.

#2253. Mourinho sends Endrick message amid Arsenal, Villa links

By Naim Beneddra | 05 Nov 2026 15:41

Real Madrid manager Jose Mourinho has admitted his frustration at being unable to give more playing time to some of his talented attackers, while offering fresh insight into Endrick's role amid continued speculation linking the Brazilian with Arsenal and Aston Villa.

The 20-year-old forward has struggled to establish himself in Mourinho's plans this season, despite returning to the Santiago Bernabeu following an impressive loan spell at Lyon.

Endrick was introduced during Wednesday's dramatic 3-2 Champions League victory over AEK Athens, featuring on the right flank as Madrid recovered from a 2-1 deficit to maintain their perfect record in the competition.

After the match, Mourinho acknowledged the difficulty of managing a squad containing so many high-quality players, while highlighting the particular threat Endrick offers against tired defenders.

His comments come at a time when the youngster's limited opportunities have once again raised questions about whether a January departure could be beneficial to his development.

Speaking after Federico Valverde's stoppage-time winner in Athens, Mourinho conceded that he found it difficult to leave talented players out of his team.

"It frustrates me for all those who are not playing because everyone deserves to play," the Portuguese coach explained, according to Real Madrid's official website.

Mourinho pointed to the performances of those who have received fewer opportunities as evidence of the considerable depth available to him.

Endrick was among the players singled out for praise, with the former Chelsea and Manchester United manager emphasising the problems his pace and directness can cause opponents.

"I think it's dramatic for a full-back, after playing an hour against someone of quality, to then face Endrick; it's truly complicated," Mourinho added.

The Brazilian replaced Bernardo Silva during the second half against AEK and provided an attacking outlet as Madrid searched for a way back into the contest.

Operating primarily from the right, Endrick created a promising opportunity for Kylian Mbappe late in the match, although the Frenchman's effort was saved.

He also saw a shot blocked during the closing stages, before Valverde secured victory with a powerful strike from outside the penalty area.

Despite those encouraging contributions, Endrick's overall involvement this season remains limited.

The former Palmeiras forward has managed only a handful of appearances and one goal since returning from France, with injuries initially delaying his return to Mourinho's matchday squad.

Competition for attacking positions has subsequently made regular minutes difficult to obtain.

Mbappe remains Madrid's principal centre-forward, while Vinicius Junior, Yan Diomande, Brahim Diaz and Carlos Espi provide further options in the attacking positions.

Mourinho nevertheless insisted that having so many alternatives was a considerable advantage rather than a problem for the team.

"There are many coaches who must look at me with a lot of healthy envy because, when I have problems, I look at the bench and have so many options," he said.

The remarks underline Mourinho's appreciation of Endrick's qualities, although they do not amount to a promise of increased playing time.

Endrick's lack of regular football has attracted attention from several European clubs ahead of the January transfer window.

Arsenal have reportedly made contact with the Brazilian's representatives over a potential move, according to TEAMtalk, with Mikel Arteta's side exploring opportunities to strengthen their attack.

The Gunners are understood to be interested in the forward's versatility and potential, although no agreement has been reported.

Aston Villa have also been linked with Endrick, having previously emerged as a potential destination for another loan spell.

Spanish radio station Cadena SER reported in October that the Birmingham club were among the leading candidates to secure his services, with Lyon and Roma also monitoring developments.

However, Mourinho has repeatedly resisted suggestions that the Brazil international should leave Madrid.

Last month, the Portuguese coach warned clubs interested in Endrick that he was not planning to sanction a January exit, describing the youngster as an important part of his squad.

That position helps explain why Wednesday's comments should not be interpreted as an indication that Madrid are preparing to negotiate his departure.

Endrick demonstrated the benefits of regular football during the second half of last season, scoring eight goals and providing eight assists in 21 appearances under Paulo Fonseca at Lyon.

His performances in France strengthened his reputation and provided valuable experience after a difficult period in Madrid.

Yet Mourinho's latest assessment suggests that he continues to see a specific role for the Brazilian within his own squad, particularly as a substitute capable of exploiting tiring defences.

Real Madrid face Valencia in La Liga on Saturday, with Mourinho confirming that further rotation will be required because of the demanding fixture schedule.

For Arsenal and Aston Villa, the central question remains whether Endrick's opportunities will increase sufficiently before January to justify another six months in Madrid.

Mourinho clearly values the youngster's attacking qualities. Whether that confidence translates into more substantial playing time may ultimately prove more important than his public praise when Endrick's future is reassessed.

#2252. West Ham fans given 'dire' Burnley warning after Scott Parker appointment

West Ham United fans have been served a warning from a Burnley perspective following the club's decision to appoint Scott Parker in place of Nuno Espírito Santo.

The Hammers dismissed the Portuguese on Tuesday morning, with Parker swiftly installed in the East London hotseat ahead of Wednesday's dramatic draw at Cardiff City.

The Irons found themselves three goals down at half-time through Jack Moylan's hat-trick, but a comeback sparked by Divine Mukasa, Arne Engels and Max Kilman's strikes saw them remain eighth, five points behind leaders Middlesbrough.

Parker's return to the club who he played 129 times for was met with mixed opinions given his managerial record, which is largely impressive at Championship level on the surface, although supporters of Fulham, AFC Bournemouth and Burnley can attest to post-promotion woes.

Football League World's Burnley fan pundit, Aaron Wright, is firmly in this camp, believing that the natives could soon get restless once more in E20, even if promotion is achieved.

Parker has won promotion to the Premier League on three occasions at his aforementioned clubs, twice finishing second whilst winning the 2020 play-off final with the Cottagers.

His immediate success at Turf Moor was met with criticism over the style of play, even if a concession of just 16 goals in 46 games in an all-time EFL record.

All three of his promotions have also come with newly-relegated teams, so when asked for his immediate thoughts on Parker's move to East London, Wright told FLW: "It's the most unsurprising news there could ever be that he's got the West Ham job. It's the biggest job he could get by an absolute mile.

"Let's be honest with his record, there was only one other job in the league he was going to take and that would be Wolves, because he's scared of every non-year one parachute team in the Championship.

"He has the best squad in the league and no doubt they will get promoted. No doubt. Whether it's winning the league, that's on Scott Parker. He's yet to do that. Yes, granted one on goal difference - it wasn't for a Plymouth defensive mistake to give Manor Solomon the ball, he would have won the Championship last time," Wright added.

"But there is also the caveat of if we hadn't have drawn 13 games, we'd have won the league. If we'd won one of them instead of drawing 13. It boggles my mind.

"You can't discredit him for also winning the number of games he had to win to cover for 13 0-0's and a few 1-1's in there. Marvellous consistency.

The new Hammers boss has generated plenty of talk for his fashion sense over time

Wright went on to admit that Parker is likely to guide West Ham back to the Premier League at the first attempt, such is the strength of the squad at his disposal, but also fired a warning to the supporters of Burnley's divisional rivals.

"West Ham will probably end up, maybe not walking this league, because I think Middlesbrough and Southampton both stand very good chances and I wouldn't want to face Birmingham in the play-offs currently. But they'll get promoted," he claimed.

"As a Burnley fan, I my thoughts are way past Scott Parker. We're having a relegation fight. I don't care what Scott Parker does now.

"But this is the biggest job he could get, if he can't get them promoted slash win the league, and if he can't then keep them in the Premier League, he needs to rethink pretty much everything. He is never getting a bigger job than this and his reputation as a player has helped him get this one. It's the most guaranteed promotion you will ever have.

"The West Ham fans won't enjoy it, but I think there'll be a good respect to it if it turns out how Burnley's did. 33 games unbeaten is something else.

"Let's not forget, this is a fanbase that saw 22 under Kompany and 23 under Dyche. To reach 33 is remarkable. We didn't lose a game from 5th November. That is insane mentality.

"But when you go up and you beat one team who was in the Premier League the year before, one, away from home because of a mad 6 minutes, we'd barely looked in a game past the end of October. We should have got better results," Wright stated.

"The football's dire. He will bore you to death, and he will cause a fanbase to turn through little snide little comments. He will cause the West Ham fanbase to turn on it. And that has happened at every single club consistently.

Burnley fans, do you agree with this viewpoint on Scott Parker? Comment below!

"There's a reason why Fulham fans and Bournemouth fans don't like him despite promotion. Burnley fans have a begrudging respect for the way he got promoted. 33 unbeaten.

"I don't know how many teams have done that at second tier level, but our defensive three and fullbacks- that three especially of Esteve, Trafford and CJ Egan-Riley -probably will go untouched for many decades in this league. And his football contributed towards that in both positive and negatives."

Given the fact West Ham are already the top scorers in the Championship, it is defensive improvements which need to be made first and foremost, with Parker's aforementioned feats at Turf Moor suggesting he could be the man to bring them to the London Stadium.

The 46-year-old was also a part of the last Hammers side to be relegated in 2011, so is also more than aware of the pressures that come with the fact a club of such stature is in the EFL to begin with.

However, that isn't the only pressure he is facing, as it was recently reported that if promotion isn't achieved in May, the board have the ability to relieve him of his duties at no cost.

#2251. Michael Carrick reacts to Man Utd boos after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick admitted Manchester United’s 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur was a painful result, after his side failed to protect the lead against 10-man Tottenham at Old Trafford.

Manchester United looked set to claim three points when Bryan Mbeumo scored in the second half off Cunha’s low cross, but Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser in injury time left Carrick facing more questions on United’s performance and his future.

Speaking to BBC Match of the Day, the United manager felt his players improved after the break but failed to handle the situation when victory was within reach.

“It was a mixed game. We found it tough to start with. For a number of reasons, we were not quite there. You could see it was a challenging time,” Carrick said.

“But the lads grew into the game, in the second half I thought we were better in everything. To get the game as we wanted it, in such a good position – we should see it through. We have to manage those situations.”

United have now conceded two late goals that have cost them valuable points, something Carrick believes has made their position look worse than it might have otherwise.

Asked whether a lack of confidence had contributed to United’s slow start, he disagreed but admitted matches can quickly slip away from his side.

The boos at half-time and full-time also clearly affected him.

“It’s not nice, it hurts a lot because I care about this club. But I understand the disappointment, the reality is what it is, we need to get back to winning games.”

#2250. Bryan Mbeumo reacts to Man Utd draw with Tottenham

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bryan Mbeumo admitted Manchester United have work to do after their frustrating 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, despite scoring a superb second-half goal at Old Trafford.

The Cameroon international put Manchester United ahead in the 73rd minute, finishing in the far corner after Matheus Cunha created the opportunity from a low cross. However, Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory, even though Tottenham had been reduced to 10 men following Rodrigo Bentancur’s red card.

Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2).

3 – Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2). Disadvantage? pic.twitter.com/DuSpvymZfu

Speaking to Sky Sports after the game, Mbeumo acknowledged that United had taken too long to find their rhythm.

“We took time to get into the game, but we played well in the second half. In the first half, that wasn’t us, we had to change. We tried to get at them,” he said.

Mbeumo recognised the threat Tottenham posed and admitted the result was disappointing, particularly given United’s position in the game before Solanke’s late goal.

“We know Spurs have good players who can be dangerous. It’s a bit disappointing, we need to work on things.”

United have struggled for consistency in the opening weeks of the season, and dropping points after taking the lead will do little to ease the pressure on Carrick. Mbeumo, however, insisted the team must look ahead rather than dwell on the setback.

“We need to keep going forward, as we have a lot of games.”

Michael Carrick was pleased with Mbeumo’s contribution, highlighting both the quality of his finish and the different roles he has played since joining United.

“Bryan’s done great. It was a fantastic finish again; he’s obviously played different roles for us but we know what he offers,” the United manager said.

“He’s got good quality and obviously the more goals he scores, the better. He’s doing really well for us.”

Mbeumo’s goal was one of the few bright moments in another difficult afternoon for United. Carrick’s team must now find a way to turn things around from here after just six points from six games.

#2249. Bolton Wanderers are giving Wrexham real transfer envy with £0 signing

Sam Dalby is fast becoming one of the more underrated strikers in the Championship today at Bolton Wanderers, which must be a real gut-punch to those associated with Wrexham.

The 26-year-old was brought into the EFL with the Red Dragons, but was a mere squad option during their National League and League Two promotion campaigns, to the point where the club decided to loan him out in the final year of his deal in the 2024/25 term.

At that point, Wrexham were a League One side, and showing no signs of slowing down. Many felt that they were rapidly outgrowing Dalby, but the towering frontman was about to prove those critics wrong.

Indeed, a brilliant loan spell at Dundee United in the Scottish Premiership made Wrexham second-guess their decision that summer, and when they tried to offer him a new deal, it was Dalby's turn to turn them down.

Now he's at Bolton, thriving, and those at the Racecourse are regretting not using him as much while he was their player.

Looking at Dalby now, it's easy to blame Wrexham for mishandling him. But it was never as easy as that. The frontman arrived at the Racecourse as a 22-year-old after a nine-goal campaign at Southend United.

He wasn't exactly pulling up trees beforehand, and he was joining a side that had Paul Mullin leading the line and Ollie Palmer beside him. It was always going to be a struggle to dislodge those from the starting eleven at National League level.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top 25 most demanding fanbases in British football right now

Dalby started just nine times in the 2022/23 campaign, but did get off the bench another 32 times. He was rarely seen as a sporadic fringe player, just not a regular starter.

That changed a little bit in League Two, though, especially in the first half of the season. Both he and Palmer rotated alongside Mullin, and he started 14 league games that year.

The only issue was that he only scored once in League Two that year, and when Wrexham went out and brought in Jack Marriott and Steven Fletcher midway through the season, he became even more of an afterthought than he was in the National League, only making two appearances in the final 13 games.

Hindsight is a brilliant thing, because there weren't many against the idea of loaning Dalby out in the summer of 2024.

After barely featuring in League Two, his minutes were only going to decrease in League One, and it seemed a given that the then-24-year-old would just see out his final 12 months under contract at Wrexham before moving on the following summer.

However, after scoring 10 goals by January 2nd in the SPL with Dundee United, who weren't expecting to be in the midst of a push for Europe in their first season back in the top flight, Wrexham quickly realised that they may have made a mistake.

Five players, five careers. Name them from the clubs they played for.

Each day there are five current or former EFL players to identify. For each one you’ll see their senior clubs in the order they played for them. Loan spells are left out.

You get four guesses per player. Every wrong guess unlocks a clue: first their position, then their nationality, then their initials.

Get it first time for 4 points, then 3, 2 and 1. The best possible score is 20. A new set of players arrives at midnight.

As stated in their retained list at the end of the 2024/25 campaign, they offered Dalby a new contract in January of that season with the hopes of extending his time at the club so they could, at the very least, profit from this breakout year.

The striker turned that down, though, and after ending the campaign with 15 goals in 35 league games, helping Dundee United finish fourth and qualify for the qualification rounds of the UEFA Conference League that year, he became a free agent.

Dalby had more than earned the four-year deal handed to him by Bolton ahead of last season. The Trotters had eyes on the Championship and, initially, the then-25-year-old was seen as a squad player to help that happen.

Like at Wrexham, Dalby was a victim of a red-hot striker in the opening months of the 2025/26 campaign, as Hull City loanee Mason Burstow scored seven in his first 11 games. In fact, the towering frontman started just four league games before mid-January, and Bolton picked up just one point in those.

From the 1-0 derby win at Wigan Athletic on January 17, something switched, and Dalby became practically undroppable. Even if Burstow started games, it was often as a two with the 26-year-old, who would be the hold-up man to bring others into the game.

He scored 10 league goals in just over 2,300 minutes, but crucially, missed the last two games through injury, which put his status in the play-offs in jeopardy.

However, that became quite the masterstroke for Steven Schumacher during their run to Wembley and their subsequent promotion. Dalby came off the bench in all three play-off matchups and changed the game in each.

Against Stockport County in the final, he came on with a little under an hour remaining, with the score at 1-1, and two minutes later, Bolton took the lead after his deft touch sent Amario Cozier-Duberry's away, and Kyle Wootton deflected the cross into his own net.

He then made it 3-1 with an overhead kick, and was, effectively, the game-changer in Bolton's promotion to the Championship.

Even despite his excellence in the play-off final, it was almost universally agreed that Bolton would need a new striker in the summer. Max Dean seemed to be the favourite, but after his move broke down, the Trotters could only really bring in more supporting members for Dalby.

The 26-year-old has thrived as the starting striker this year, though. He's continued to be one of the best linkmen in the EFL and has more than a handful of goals to show for it, too.

How many goals do you think Sam Dalby will score in the Championship this season? Let us know in the comments.

And, crucially, at this moment in time, he's scored more league goals this season than anyone in the Wrexham squad. For what is his first season in the second tier, it's not been bad going so far.

Now, he's one of Bolton's most important players, and one that you would imagine needs to remain fit for the Trotters to stand a chance at establishing themselves in the Championship, which isn't something anyone associated with Wrexham would have imagined back in the summer of 2024 after loaning him out to Dundee United.

#2248. Portsmouth vs Stoke City - prediction, team news, lineups

By Ben Sully | 05 Nov 2026 15:52, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 20:34

Two in-form sides will face off when Portsmouth welcome Stoke City to Fratton Park for Saturday's Championship encounter.

Pompey head into the weekend in 10th position in the Championship table, while the Potters are in fourth spot and a point adrift of automatic promotion.

Portsmouth share the league's current longest unbeaten run with rivals Southampton after winning four and drawing two of their last six matches.

Pompey continued their impressive run of form with a dramatic 3-1 victory over promotion hopefuls Swansea in Tuesday's clash at Fratton Park.

After seeing his opener cancelled out by Zan Vipotnik's 85th-minute penalty, Terry Devlin put his team back in front in the second minute of stoppage time, shortly before Thomas Waddingham netted in a second straight home game to wrap up all three points.

As a result, Pompey, who have battled relegation in the last two seasons, are sitting just one point adrift from the top eight and four points away from automatic promotion, sparking dreams of ending their 16-year wait for Premier League football.

They will go into Saturday's fixture with hopes of recording four consecutive home league wins for the first time since January 2025.

The fourth win on that occasion came via a 2-1 scoreline against Stoke, which may be a good omen as they look to extend their five-game unbeaten home streak.

Stoke, meanwhile, have lost just one of their last 11 Championship outings, representing a significant improvement from starting the campaign with three consecutive league defeats.

In fact, they have won three of their last four outings since suffering their most recent loss in a home clash against Middlesbrough last month.

The Potters recorded clean sheets in wins against Derby County and Preston North End, either side of a goalless draw against Preston North End.

Mark Robins's side missed out on a fourth consecutive clean sheet in their midweek away clash against Blackburn Rovers, but crucially they left Ewood Park with all three points after claiming a 2-1 victory thanks to a Jack McGlynn effort and an own goal.

That result represented a fourth consecutive win on the road and made it five consecutive away games without defeat since losing to Wolverhampton Wanderers at the end of August.

The Potters enjoyed success on their last trip to Fratton Park in October 2025, when a 70th-minute own goal proved enough to fire them to a 1-0 victory.

Portsmouth remain without Benjamin Arthur, Connor Ogilvie, Mark Kosznovszky, John Swift, Luke Brooke-Smith, Keshi Anderson and Colby Bishop.

Striker Marko Milovanovic has been ruled out of Saturday’s contest after he pulled up with a quad issue in the second half against Swansea.

Midfielder Ilyas Mohamed was also forced off with a calf issue in Tuesday’s win, and while it is believed to be a minor problem, it is unclear whether he will be ready for the weekend.

As for the Potters, they head to Fratton Park without the injured trio of Eric Bocat, Lamine Cisse and Ato Ampah.

Aaron Cresswell and Sam Gallagher are on the verge of returning from injury, but it remains to be seen whether they will prove their fitness in time for Saturday’s away trip.

One certainty is that midfielder Ethan Galbraith will return to the fold after serving a one-match ban for an accumulation of yellow cards.

#2247. Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp tear into Man Utd after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have come under fresh criticism after their disappointing 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, with Sky Sports pundits Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp questioning the team’s performance and the challenges facing Michael Carrick.

Manchester United looked on course for a much-needed victory at Old Trafford after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half. However, despite Tottenham being reduced to 10 men, Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser denied Carrick’s side all three points.

Gary Neville felt Tottenham deserved the points, pointing to Solanke’s late goal and his impact as Tottenham captain.

“Tottenham deserved a point. That was a really important moment for Solanke. He’d missed some big chances and was captain for the first time,” Neville said on Sky Sports.

“They were the better team by far. If they’d left without a point, it would have been terrible for them.”

The former United defender also expressed concern about what the result means for Carrick, who faces increasing pressure to improve performances and results.

“Michael Carrick is left with a lot to think about. It was a worrying day in terms of the level of performance and a big week ahead.”

Manchester United spent massively in the summer to overhaul their midfield. However, Jamie Redknapp was critical of the performance he saw on the pitch.

“I do not see enough connections in that midfield, and they look devoid of any confidence, ideas, or patterns of play, playing out the back,” Redknapp said.

“Tottenham were just much better than them in every department, but that is a theme this season.”

The former Liverpool midfielder also questioned United’s energy and attacking organization, suggesting the squad is not strong enough to meet the demands placed upon it.

“Their energy levels are not good enough, and they look loose going forward; there was no real structure.”

Redknapp added that Carrick cannot fix every issue alone, insisting the manager needs backing to improve the squad.

“The squad is not good or strong enough, and Michael [Carrick] needs help in that respect.”

#2246. ‘Embarrassing themselves!’ Jamie Carragher hits back at Rio Ferdinand over Cristiano Ronaldo defence

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Carragher has taken aim at Rio Ferdinand for defending Cristiano Ronaldo following the Portuguese forward’s latest controversy with his national team.

Ronaldo has found himself at the centre of a growing dispute after abruptly leaving Portugal’s national team camp following a reported disagreement with head coach Jorge Jesus. The incident has prompted strong reactions from former teammates and pundits, with Ferdinand firmly siding with the Al-Nassr star.

Jamie Carragher, however, believes Ronaldo’s reputation should not shield him from criticism when his behaviour becomes difficult to defend.

Rio Ferdinand, who played alongside Cristiano Ronaldo at Manchester United between 2003 and 2009, criticised Jorge Jesus for the way he handled the situation.

Speaking on his Rio Presents podcast, the former United captain criticised Jesus for not showing respect to Cristiano Ronaldo.

“Cristiano Ronaldo was put in a very bad situation and was treated in an inappropriate way. He has an enormous history in football. Jorge Jesus did not deal with Ronaldo well and chose to treat him in that way, but I cannot find any explanation for it,” Ferdinand said.

Despite Jesus being Portugal’s manager, Ferdinand questioned his standing, comparing him with the likes of Sir Alex Ferguson and Pep Guardiola. To say the least, Ferdinand only embarrassed himself doing that.

Jamie Carragher saw the argument very differently and rightfully so.

Speaking on The Overlap Daily, the former Liverpool defender suggested that Ferdinand’s friendship with Ronaldo was influencing his reaction to the incident.

“People think it’s good to be close to people in the game, but this is where it becomes a problem,” Carragher explained.

He then called out both Ferdinand and broadcaster Piers Morgan, who has also been vocal in his support of Ronaldo.

“People like Piers Morgan and Rio Ferdinand, who are big people in the media whether you like them or not, are embarrassing themselves with the nonsense that they’re coming out with because they know Ronaldo. You can know someone and try to look after them, but you cannot defend the indefensible.”

Carragher believes Ronaldo’s achievements should not put the forward beyond criticism, particularly when his actions involve his national team and its management.

Gary Neville, who also played with Cristiano Ronaldo, has questioned Ronaldo’s handling of the situation, despite his longstanding affection for his former Manchester United teammate.

Neville argued that Ronaldo’s public response had made matters worse and the forward needed to accept criticism rather than expect others to defend him.

“With Cristiano, it’s difficult because I love him, but I can’t watch what happened at United in his second period and then see him make a statement like that without saying something. He has to be pulled up on that and told that it isn’t acceptable,” Neville said.

He also suggested Ronaldo’s extraordinary success, wealth and influence could have contributed to a sense that he was beyond challenge.

“When you have a billion pounds in the bank, the achievements he has had and the power he has, you feel you’re unbreakable, you feel you’re unstoppable,” Neville added.

Despite his criticism, Neville does not believe the episode will define Ronaldo’s legacy permanently. He expects the attention to fade once the forward retires, allowing his achievements on the pitch to take centre stage again.

#2245. AI predicts Sheffield Wednesday's League One finish under Dave Challinor

Sheffield Wednesday have their man, as Dave Challinor was officially confirmed as the new Owls boss earlier this week.

The 51-year-old seemed almost destined for the post at Hillsborough ever since his hugely successful stint at Stockport County came to an end following defeat in the play-off final against Bolton Wanderers.

County's CEO Simon Wilson departed at the end of the season to become the new sporting director at Wednesday, and now the two will work together once more in South Yorkshire to get over the line and into the second tier after falling short at Stockport.

The Hatters finished third in consecutive League One campaigns under Challinor, who'd overseen their rise from the National League to the third tier in the four-and-a-half years he spent in charge.

Five players, five careers. Name them from the clubs they played for.

Each day there are five current or former EFL players to identify. For each one you’ll see their senior clubs in the order they played for them. Loan spells are left out.

You get four guesses per player. Every wrong guess unlocks a clue: first their position, then their nationality, then their initials.

Get it first time for 4 points, then 3, 2 and 1. The best possible score is 20. A new set of players arrives at midnight.

Promotion will be the goal, and this demanding fanbase, who called for Henrik Pedersen's head despite sitting eighth in the division at the time, will be wanting it at the quickest possible moment, but what does AI think?

Henrik Pedersen's dismissal came after just 11 games of the League One campaign. At that time, Wednesday were two points removed from a play-off place with a game in hand, and had won games 7-2 and 5-0 against Bromley and AFC Wimbledon, respectively.

Ultimately, Challinor isn't coming in to a club in crisis, even if just two points in a run of games against Notts County, Doncaster Rovers, Cambridge United and Blackpool isn't exactly play-off form.

The ex-Stockport boss has 33 league games to help gain, at worst, two points on the side in sixth place, or six points on his former side, who occupy the second automatic promotion spot, which seems a doable task.

According to ChatGPT, he'll succeed in the former, but fall just short of finishing in the top two. Indeed, AI feels that Sheffield Wednesday will finish 4th this season under Challinor.

Do you think Sheffield Wednesday can get promoted this season under Dave Challinor? Let us know in the comments.

While that wouldn't be the ideal scenario for some Owls fans, they can feel reassured that ChatGPT went on to state that it predicts play-off success at Wembley, with Challinor's recent history of struggling to get through the postseason at Stockport not bleeding into his tenure at Hillsborough.

Ultimately, the appointment of Dave Challinor won't just mean that Sheffield Wednesday will stroll up the League One table and finish in the top two with ease. The 51-year-old has good credentials, but even he knows how unforgiving this division can be.

Additionally, this is a job like no other for Challinor. The Owls are a giant of English football that, ultimately, the likes of AFC Fylde, Hartlepool United and Stockport aren't.

Especially at Stockport, there wasn't as much fan pressure if things weren't going as well. Ultimately, he's brought the club closer to the Championship than they have been for 25 years, and that gave him plenty of credit in the bank.

At Sheffield Wednesday, though, he'll be under pressure to oversee winning performances in front of 30,000 at Hillsborough every other week and packed away ends elsewhere, and if bad results creep in, as they did under Pedersen, it won't take long for negativity to set in.

This is a job that Challinor has earned, but there's still a tiny bit of scepticism surrounding his fit from AI, hence the haste in putting them amongst the top two immediately.

Challinor was in the stands at Hillsborough on Sunday in their draw against Blackpool, observing and writing notes. He'll have plenty of time to get to work and implement some of those ideas in the coming weeks.

Wednesday are in action against Oldham Athletic in the FA Cup first round on Sunday in front of the TV cameras, giving the Owls a chance to showcase any changes immediately in front of a national audience.

From then, though, they have an EFL Trophy matchup at Accrington Stanley, which will serve almost as a free hit with the Owls already through to the knockout phase, and then likely a nine-day break due to the upcoming international window.

AI has had its say on which clubs are currently the biggest sleeping giants outside the Premier League

Dave Challinor's side haven't had their game against Plymouth Argyle postponed just yet, but it will likely happen. Kieran Morrison has already been called up for Northern Ireland, and their two games over the last break had to be rearranged, too.

So, with only one "meaningful" game against an out-of-form League Two opponent in his first 17 days officially in charge, it presents a good opportunity to stamp his authority on this new side.

All eyes will be on Oakwell on November 20, as Wednesday travel to Barnsley in what could set the tone for a promotion charge. Perhaps then, a fourth-placed prediction could be selling them a little short.

#2244. Is Mainoo-Tielemans the right midfield option for Man Utd?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United drew 1-1 at home to Tottenham despite having an extra man advantage. The Reds saw Mbeumo put them in front, but Solanke scored a late leveller in injury time after Bentancur was shown a red card for headbutting Luke Shaw.

Manchester United’s worrisome season continues after accumulating just six points off the first six games, their first start in 40 years. But more than the results, what worries Manchester United is their midfield pairing.

United spent massively on their midfield rebuild and somehow can’t find the best combination in the midfield. Michael Carrick has gone with the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership, but they have failed to control the midfield. Individually, they both come with decent numbers, but collectively they fall short.

Mainoo and Tielemans struggled against Tottenham while closing the midfield and at times showed a lack of energy. On the day when nothing clicked for United, the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership particularly raised eyebrows.

Gary Neville has also raised serious concerns about Manchester United’s Mainoo-Tielemans partnership.

Neville was particularly unimpressed by the midfield balance, arguing that the problems went beyond one disappointing performance.

The former United captain stopped short of ruling out the combination altogether, but he suggested he has serious doubts about its suitability.

“Tielemans and Mainoo, I’m not calling time on it, that’s probably too drastic, but I’ve seen enough of it to think that’s not a midfield that’s dynamic enough to play at this football club in the Premier League.”

Former Spurs midfielder Redknapp felt United struggled to connect their midfield with the rest of the team during the opening stages.

He added: “I didn’t see enough connections in that midfield in the first hour. Baleba came on and did OK and showed a bit more.

“They look devoid of any confidence, any idea, any patterns of play, playing out from the back. Tottenham were much better than them in every single department.”

While Carlos Baleba was a particular highlight on the day, he showed great potential when brought on. He brought a new sense of energy in the midfield and sent a few balls over the top for a couple of quick counterattacks as well.

Redknapp also pointed to the physical demands of modern Premier League football, arguing that United cannot afford to be outworked by their opponents.

Tottenham ran a staggering 5 km more than Manchester United, something Redknapp felt was a pattern.

"Spurs ran 5km more than Man United… it's like that EVERY GAME!"Jamie Redknapp is less than impressed with the lack of ground covered this season by Manchester United 😬 pic.twitter.com/BxWkFuyGJ1

“There is a pattern that I don’t really like when I watch teams, and that’s when every time the stats don’t lie and the other team runs more than you.

“I feel in Premier League football right now there are a couple of non-negotiables, and one is you have to run more than the other team if you want to win or at least get close enough. Tonight Spurs ran 5km more than Manchester United.”

He believes Tottenham’s pressing exposed the lack of pace in United’s midfield, making it difficult for the hosts to move the ball safely through the centre.

“In terms of how they played today, I think they have an issue with Tielemans and Mainoo in midfield. I don’t see enough energy in there. Baleba will add a lot and will give them a tremendous amount of energy but right now, when things aren’t going well… and Tottenham knew it!

“Every time United played the ball out from the back they pounced. They pressed them because they knew United don’t have the pace and speed in midfield to get away.”

“In isolation, one of them is absolutely fine, the two of them together doesn’t work for me. They haven’t got the pace or the energy to get around the pitch for 90 minutes.”

Cardiff City and Burnley square off on Saturday afternoon in what could prove to be a pivotal game in the battle to avoid relegation to League One.

While the Bluebirds sit in 18th position in the Championship table, Burnley are in 22nd spot, already four points adrift of safety.

With eight points from the last five games, Cardiff boss Brian Barry-Murphy can only be delighted with such a return.

The Bluebirds have played Watford, Middlesbrough, Birmingham City, Wolverhampton Wanderers and West Ham United in succession.

However, squandering a three-goal half-time lead against the latter of those teams would have hurt Barry-Murphy, regardless of West Ham benefitting from the arrival of new boss Scott Parker.

In the grand scheme of things, however, Cardiff are five points above the relegation zone and have put together a seven-match unbeaten run at the Cardiff City Stadium.

Trailing 12th place by just three points, the Welsh outfit are still in contention to launch a surprise playoff bid.

At the same time, defeat to Burnley on Saturday would see Cardiff only sit above the relegation zone by two points.

Fabio Celestini has got Burnley back on track with two respectable draws against Portsmouth and Queens Park Rangers.

While the well-travelled Italian knows that the Clarets need to deliver just a second Championship win of the season as soon as possible, there have at least been positives to take from his opening double header.

Burnley have collected five points from their last five away fixtures, arguably an encouraging return for a squad that has struggled to gel.

With Cardiff right-back Perry Ng suffering an injury in the closing stages against West Ham, Ronan Kpakio is in line to deputise.
