Barring any fresh injury concerns, Celestini may stick with the Burnley XI from the draw at QPR.

However, Josh Cullen, Aaron Ramsey and Jaydon Banel are all options in midfield and the final third.

Bashir Humphreys, Hannibal Mejbri and Chelsea loanee Dastan Satpaev remain on the sidelines.

#2242. Celtic revival or one-off resistance and is Duran the best player in Scotland?

Welcome to The Burning Questions, our new daily debate where we take one major subject in Scottish sport and tackle the three questions everyone is talking about.

Each day, our writers will offer their verdicts on the biggest issues and arguments dominating the agenda. And Celtic are back under the spotlight after Martin O’Neill’s side delivered a much-needed European victory over Besiktas.

O’Neill appeared visibly emotional at the full-time whistle after watching his team produce an inspired display, but was this the start of a genuine turnaround or simply a singular act of resistance in a difficult season?

Read more: Who says Martin O'Neill is out of touch? Besiktas win was a tactical masterclass

Then there’s Camilo Duran. Celtic’s recruitment has taken plenty of criticism, but the £5.5million Colombian continues to deliver and his latest goals have strengthened his claim to be the best player in Scottish football right now.

Read more: Who says Martin O'Neill is out of touch? Besiktas win was a tactical masterclass

And what about Europe? Despite their setbacks, Celtic’s hopes of reaching the Europa League knockout play-off round remain alive. Can O’Neill’s men build on the Besiktas win and collect enough points from their remaining four games to squeeze into the top 24?

Matthew Lindsay and Graeme Young give their answers to today’s three Burning Questions.

Matthew Lindsay: Time will tell. The 7-1 mauling of Hearts last month certainly proved to be a false dawn for the Scottish champions. Defeats against Celta Vigo and Falkirk and a draw with Dundee United followed that impressive result.

Celtic were excellent against a very good Besiktas team last night. They contained opponents who had beaten Hoffenheim and Marseille and drawn with Crystal Palace extremely well, while creating a raft of scoring chances in the final half-hour. It was a deserved victory and they should have prevailed by a more comfortable margin.

But I still fear Celtic are short of the quality they need in key areas. They desperately need a physical, defensively-minded central midfielder and I suspect their lack of strength in depth will prevent them from going on an extended winning run in the weeks ahead.

That said, I didn’t expect them to produce such an inspired display against Vincenzo Italiano’s men. The performance lifted the Celtic supporters and the players can tap into that feelgood factor going forward.

Graeme Young: I have a feeling this might still prove to be the start of a roaring second half of the season that defies convention. There was every reason to be perplexed by Celtic’s starting XI, but naming the likes of Kasper Hogh and Sebastien Tounekti allowed thrilling talents such as Camilo Duran and Callum Osmand to enter the game as if they had been fired out of a cannon.

But it would be remiss to downplay Celtic’s patterns of play, or lack thereof, in the biggest matches. Besiktas turned out to be a bit of a Turkey. It’s unlikely the better teams they face will be quite so welcoming as the fixtures continue to pile up.

Matthew Lindsay: Yes. For me, the Colombian is the form player in the country just now. His brace against Besiktas took his tally for the 2026/27 campaign to 13. Considering he has often been played out of position out wide, it is some return. He has been £5.5million very well spent indeed.

There is still an awfully long way to go before the Player of the Year awards are doled out and much can change. But if Celtic improve at home and abroad in the weeks and months ahead, and their midweek win augurs well for the future, then Duran will have even more scoring chances.

There have been a few standouts in Scottish football this term. Josh Fowler at St Johnstone is the leading marksman in the top flight, Ryan Naderi at Rangers has been inspired at times and Josh McPake has forced his way into the Scotland set-up. But the former Qarabag forward is the pick of the bunch.

Graeme Young: Yes. Rangers star defender Olwethu Makhanya looks every inch a superstar in the making, but this beautiful old game of ours is ultimately decided by the players who put the ball in the net.

Duran brings goals, versatility and an uncanny knack for making the difference in the biggest moments. His connection with the aforementioned Osmand is electric and feels like Celtic’s best shot at getting themselves firing in the right direction.

Matthew Lindsay: The Scottish champions' fine win over Besiktas moved them to within a point of the clubs occupying the knockout play-off spots in the Europa League league phase table. But going through remains a tall order.

They have three difficult away matches against Torreense, Omonia Nicosia and Royal Union Saint-Gilloise coming up in the months ahead. They will need to win on the road to progress.

Second-tier Portuguese outfit Torreense, who are up next in three weeks, showed they will not be easily overcome once again last night by beating Ararat-Armenia at home to move to six points.

If Celtic maintain the levels they hit last night, they could do it. But that is a big if. I fear their opening losses to Ferencvaros, Benfica and Celta Vigo may come back to haunt them.

Graeme Young: Do you know what? They might just do it.

O’Neill landed wins against Feyenoord and Utrecht, as well as a point in Bologna, during the second half of their Europa League campaign last season and looks a good bet to do something similar again.

Torreense, the Portuguese second-tier side, feels like a danger mission. Damned if you do, condemned if you don’t. Omonia doesn’t look the most challenging assignment and then it’s Marseille, who are also toiling, before Celtic finish against Union Saint-Gilloise, who may already be safely inside the top eight by the time they face the Premiership champions.

If Celtic land seven points from their final four games, it might just be enough to squeeze them into the top 24. But only just.

#2241. Tottenham's hidden West Ham, Manor Solomon clause takes new twist

West Ham's decision to acquire Manor Solomon on a permanent transfer from Tottenham Hotspur over the summer raised initial eyebrows and is now coming under intensifying scrutiny, leaving questions to be asked over both his long-term future and a certain clause within the deal.

The Hammers' reintroduction to life in the Championship following 14 largely-successful years at Premier League level has not quite followed the script, having headed into the campaign as overwhelming title favourites.

West Ham find themselves positioned in eighth place after 14 games, however, and recently relieved Nuno Espirito Santo of his duties in the wake of successive defeats against two promotion rivals in Southampton and West Bromwich Albion before appointing Scott Parker.

The ex-Hammers and England international midfielder's tenure got off to a shaky start on Tuesday evening when the East Londoners could only muster a 3-3 draw away to Cardiff City, albeit with a remarkable recovery from a shock 3-0 deficit at half time, and it will be interesting to see what direction of travel they enjoy under the stewardship of a three-time second-tier promotion-winner with serious skin in the game within the club.

And exactly what lies ahead for Solomon, who has predominantly fallen short of delivering the desired impact upon his switch across London, only remains to be seen, putting the microscope on the deal that took the winger to the Championship in the first place.

West Ham shelled out an initial £5 million figure to secure Solomon's services on a permanent basis and agreed a three-year contract with the former Leeds United loan star, with the deal capable of rising to £7 million subject to various add-on criteria.

It's only likely, of course, that some criteria pertains to the Hammers' ongoing promotion charge, and though they are only just inside the play-offs at this moment in time, it's hard to look past Scott Parker's men making up relative ground as the campaign toils on and achieving a top-two finish.

Another clause, however, is far more interesting, and provides an intriguing discussion point amid increasing criticism of Solomon's acquisition in East London.

It was widely reported that Spurs, where the 27-year-old wide-man made just six competitive appearances across an injury-plagued spell of three years and just as many loans away from the club, included a 10% sell-on clause for any future fee that West Ham collect further down the line.

These seven players won't be available as Scott Parker looks for his first win as West Ham boss against Blackburn

Solomon's resale value, mind you, is open to debate, and his stock has declined after previously lighting up the Championship on loan at Leeds to inspire their 100-point title triumph with 10 goals and 12 assists during the 2024/25 campaign.

With the West Ham attacker struggling to find his rhythm in the opening months, he's only earning the ever-growing ire of the London Stadium faithful while posing questions about his price-tag, the club's decision to complete the signing and exactly what Spurs could end up getting out of their sell-on clause.

Solomon has previous pedigree in European competition with Shakhtar Dontesk and tangible top-flight experience from spells at Fulham, Villarreal and Fiorentina alongside a strong second-tier reputation from his aforementioned exploits in West Yorkshire, but none of that has been translated to claret colours since making the move to West Ham.

He started off in style by finding the back of the net on debut during West Ham's 2-2 opening-weekend draw at Burnley before adding a goal and an assist to his name when the Hammers ran riot over Wrexham in a 6-0 rout on September 11.

Solomon would then set up another in West Ham's next league fixture away to fierce rivals Millwall a week later, though it's now been a month-and-a-half - or six matches - since Solomon last provided a direct goal involvement.

For a player of the ex-Shakhtar and Spurs winger's pedigree, the lack of production has left Hammers supporters frustrated as he continues to flatter to deceive.

Notably off the pace in his side's most recent draw at Cardiff, in which he lasted 70 minutes before being hauled off for £18 million summer signing Edvin Austbo as the visitors chased an all-important equaliser, the signing is now at real risk of not working out for all parties - leaving many to wonder why Spurs even added a sell-on clause in the first place.

Unfortunately for Solomon, it would appear as though West Ham fans have already made their minds up and the winger now faces a losing battle to regain the support of the London Stadium crowd.

His anonymous display against the Bluebirds has seemingly provided the final nail in the coffin for some, with many supporters taking to social media in recent times to make their thoughts clear.

This one fan, who was far from the only punter to share such a sentiment, doesn't wish to see the ex-Spurs wide-man in a Hammers shirt again.

He's far from popular in East London right now, which was displayed here.

Some would argue this particular claim is too harsh, although it's a damning indictment of just how unpopular Solomon has become merely months after joining the promotion-chasing side.

West Ham fans, do you think there's any way back for Manor Solomon? What have you made of the winger's start to life in East London? Let us know your thoughts in the comments below!

Solomon still has time to turn it around, of course, but many West Ham supporters don't want him to be given that and have even urged for his contract to be ripped up already.

This fan hasn't been as vocal about Solomon, but is still onside with the general consensus that Austbo, having arrived as one of the most expensive signings in Championship history, deserves a run of games ahead of his left-flank counterpart.

The pacey Norweigan was much livelier against Cardiff after coming on for the closing stages and has the same number of goal contributions - two goals and two assists apiece - in 667 fewer minutes of league action.

Indeed, Austbo has started on just two occasions as Solomon continues to fall short of providing the goods as a consistent starter, and it only remains to be seen whether Parker will take to the tricky 21-year-old more than his predecessor.

West Ham fans will hope that's the case, though, and if Austbo seizes any forthcoming opportunities for a regular run in the side then it's hard to see how Solomon will make good of his transfer and become any sort of successful signing under Parker.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.
